Now a published candidate that cannot be forwarded immediately **joins a small priority queue instead of being dropped**, and each slot the cap grants is spent on the highest-priority pending candidate. **THE SELECTION RULE:** (1) per-strategy score **quantile band** (quintile of that strategy's own day distribution), descending — *never* the raw score, because `score` semantics are per-scanner and orb's 0.9 is not the same statement as rsi2's 0.9, whereas "top of what this strategy produced today" means the same thing for every strategy; (2) inside one band, `fired_at` **ascending** — comparable standing means the older setup goes first, since it is the one closest to going stale; (3) arrival sequence ascending as the final tie-break, so the order is total and identical on replay. A queued candidate is a REFUSED one we kept a pointer to: it expires at its own §5.2 TTL horizon (a 90-minute-old breakout level is not the setup the scanner saw) and expiry does **not** re-arm its day slot — the forward cap has never re-armed (2026-07-29). A governor budget block still neither re-arms nor queues (a blocked window must not build a backlog that hammers the admission gate when the block lifts). **Trigger (c) heartbeat and trigger (b) position events never consume a forward slot** — they are governor-metered only, and the cap exists to bound *candidate evaluations*. **The forward counter is journalled** into `prescreen_day_slots.forwarded` (a counter, not a flag: a pair re-armed after an analyst infrastructure failure can legitimately be forwarded again and each attempt is real spend), so a mid-day restart RESUMES the day's analyst quota instead of refilling it — the same defect the 2026-08-04 journal fixed for the publication caps, which had stayed open on the more expensive of the two bounds. Rollback: `strategy.prescreen.admission_mode: arrival` (first-come-first-served).

> **Ranking needs something to rank: the drain is PACED (§5.2 trigger (a), 2026-08-14).** WO-1 shipped the selection rule but left the trigger on the arrival path — a candidate was enqueued and then immediately drained — so at every slot the queue held exactly one entry, its own, and "forward the highest-score pending candidate" was satisfied *vacuously*. First live day under it (2026-08-14): all 12 slots consumed by ~09:20, five minutes into the session, on `rsi2` scores `[0.14, 0.17, 0.27, 0.46, 0.48, 0.66]`, while the day's best unforwarded candidates (`rsi2` 0.83, `orb` 1.00, `mom` 1.00) arrived later and never got one — the analyst then declined the entire lot as structurally premature. Across a day, early-low beat late-high. **Now: an arriving candidate ONLY enqueues.** A drain tick — one-minute scheduler pulse, gated to one drain per `FORWARD_PACING_MIN` (3 min) — spends at most **one** slot on the best pending candidate by the same key, so each slot chooses among everything that accumulated inside its interval. One per tick, never a burst: draining the remaining budget the moment it is available is the inline drain again, just batched. The drain re-checks every entry-path gate (mode, risk state, kill, window, governor, cap) because time has passed since the candidate was queued; a closed gate skips the tick and leaves the queue intact (no re-arm — expiry via the candidate's own TTL remains its only exit). The pacing interval is bounded from three sides: above the 60 s pulse, well under `TTL_INTRADAY_MIN` (20) so a queued candidate gets several chances before its levels go stale, and cap × pacing must fit the trade window (12 × 3 = 36 min at DG0, 4 × 3 = 12 min at DG1+). Rollback: `strategy.prescreen.forward_drain_mode: immediate`. **Auditability (same day, reviewer-flagged):** the §5.5 funnel section stops at *evaluated*, so 12 evaluated → 0 proposals was a decision the review could not see; it now carries an **analyst-declines block** — evaluated/declined/proposed, declines per strategy, and the day's five most recent `no_action` reasons — read from `agent_calls` (identity recovered from the archived prompt, since a `no_action` payload carries none).

### 5.3 Pre-open Planner

| | |
|---|---|
| Job | 08:50 daily: produce the day plan — regime read, 3–8 focus symbols from the universe with reasoning, levels to watch, event warnings (results days, expiry, ex-dates), explicitly grounded in pre-open auction mechanics: indicative pre-open prices are auction artifacts, the real open prints at 09:15 via auction; plan entries accordingly and never off pre-open indicative ticks (A14) |
| Trigger | Scheduled, trading days only (R6) |
| Model | Sonnet 4.6, single-shot, no tools; `max_output_tokens=2000` |
| Context | overnight bhavcopy movers, gap scan vs prior close, **the catalyst digest + graded watchlist (§2.7 step 5 — sector/theme/market sentiment lines + per-symbol catalyst entries with their deterministic levels)**, earnings calendar today, surveillance-list changes (A8), open positions + their overnight risk, yesterday's review summary |
| Output schema | `DayPlan{regime, focus:[{symbol, bias, levels, why}], catalyst_focus:[{symbol, event_type, direction, advisory_levels, drift_note}], warnings:[], no_trade_today: bool}` — persisted as one SQLite row per trading day (table `day_plans(date PRIMARY KEY, payload JSON, created_at)`, distinct from the raw agent-call audit record), fed into intraday contexts (stable block, D8). **`catalyst_focus` is advisory commentary on watchlist entries — the binding origination levels remain the scanner's deterministic ones (§2.7 step 6); the planner can highlight/deprioritize but cannot add a symbol to the watchlist or upgrade a `context` entry to `originating`** |

### 5.4 News Analyst (formerly "Sentiment Scorer"; §2.7 step 4)

| | |
|---|---|
| Job | Score queued headline **clusters** (§3.2.4): per-cluster `{scope: market\|sector\|theme\|stock, entities[] (verbatim strings — resolved to symbols ONLY by EntityResolver, never by the LLM, §2.7 step 3), sectors[], themes[] (from the theme_map vocabulary supplied in-prompt), sentiment −1..+1, materiality 0..1 (rubric-anchored), event_type (closed taxonomy below), novelty 0..1 (is this NEW information vs rehash of an already-scored story)}` (E6 — own LLM scoring, not GDELT tone) |
| Event taxonomy (closed enum — schema-validated; out-of-enum ⇒ cluster dropped + logged, D7) | `earnings_result, earnings_guidance, order_win, capacity_expansion, m_and_a, mgmt_change, regulatory_policy, govt_program, rating_change, analyst_action, legal_action, dividend_corp_action, macro_data, global_market, sector_policy, disruption, pump_promo_suspect, other`. The **originating subset** (the only event types eligible for an `originating` watchlist grade) is owner-fixed in `limits.yaml catalyst_guard.originating_event_types` — default `{earnings_result, earnings_guidance, order_win, m_and_a, regulatory_policy, govt_program, sector_policy, rating_change}`; `pump_promo_suspect` and `other` **never** originate (§2.4/§2.7) |
| Materiality rubric (anchored in-prompt) | ≥0.8 company-transforming (order ≥ ~10% of annual revenue, M&A, direct regulatory action on the company, major govt program naming the sector) **OR** a market-wide shock (country-level tariff/trade-policy action, central-bank rate decision, escalating geopolitical conflict, macro data surprise — PMI, inflation, FII/DII flow reversal — expected to move the broad market or many sectors at once); 0.5–0.8 clearly price-relevant to the stock, sector or market; 0.2–0.5 routine coverage; <0.2 noise/PR. One score per cluster |
| Trigger | Batch of ≥8 clusters or 30 min elapsed with queue non-empty; market hours + 19:00–22:00 sweep + **the pre-open batch, which must complete before the ~08:35 digest (§4.4 job 14)** — if late, the digest runs with whatever is scored (unscored clusters are excluded from origination; fails to zero, §2.7) |
| Model | **Haiku 4.5**; single-shot, no tools; batched ≤30 clusters/call; priced 100% cache-miss (stable prefix ~2.5k < Haiku's 4,096-token minimum cacheable prefix, D8 — the *prefix*, not the whole ~5.5k prompt, is what falls under the cache minimum) |
| Pre-open batch rule | fires when startup news backfill + clustering + resolution complete **or at 08:15, whichever is later**; scores ALL unscored clusters regardless of the ≥8 minimum; must finish before the ~08:35 digest (late ⇒ digest runs with what is scored) |
| Failure | Scores absent ⇒ clusters stay unscored ⇒ excluded from the watchlist (`cat` originates nothing — §2.7 fail-safe ladder); analyst context marks "sentiment unavailable" (D7) — never blocks anything else |

### 5.5 Nightly Post-Trade Reviewer & Weekly Strategy Researcher

*Phase-2 v1 deviation (2026-07-28): the nightly reviewer ships SINGLE-SHOT over a deterministically pre-assembled context (day's ledger/recommendation/verdict/agent-failure/budget summary) — at Phase-2 volume the agentic tool loop adds cost and an unverified SDK MCP surface for no analytical gain. The output contract below is unchanged, so the agentic shape + read-only MCP toolset return in Phase 3 as a drop-in; `run_budget_tokens` in `agents.yaml` is retained as that upgrade's envelope. The weekly researcher is parked (`enabled: false`) until Phase 5.*

| | Nightly Reviewer | Weekly Researcher |
|---|---|---|
| Job | Review the day: every closed trade vs thesis (from learning ledger §6.5), attribution incl. ex-date/flagged-day/`catalyst_ref` tags (A12/§2.7), process errors, gate rejections; emit structured lessons + envelope-parameter *suggestions* (→ validation pipeline §6.4, never applied directly, R4) | Deep weekly review: strategy performance vs baselines, regime analysis, champion/challenger recommendations, *proposed* strategy-logic changes (always owner-approval, R4); **the §2.7 suggestion loop: propose `entity_aliases` / `theme_map` additions from the unresolved-entity/theme logs (owner approves via dashboard — platform suggests, owner sets, §6.3) and review catalyst-watchlist precision** |
| Trigger | 21:00 trading days (aligned to `agents.yaml` 2026-07-07; §10.1) | Sunday 18:00 (aligned to `agents.yaml` 2026-07-07 — after the Sunday sector/theme-map job, §4.4.13) |
| Model & caps | Sonnet 4.6, **agentic**, `max_turns=12` (`agents.yaml`), per-call `max_output_tokens` 6k; total **billed** cap 120k in/10k out tokens (governor-enforced run budget from `agents.yaml run_budget_tokens` — the per-call knob and the cumulative cap are different levers) | Opus 4.8 (D9), agentic, `max_turns=20` (`agents.yaml`), per-call `max_output_tokens` 10k; billed cap 200k in/16k out (`run_budget_tokens`) |
| Tools | Read-only MCP tools over the platform's own data: `query_ledger`, `query_bars`, `query_decisions`, `query_news` — no filesystem, no network, no order surface | same + `query_backtest` — an in-engine asyncio job queue (same process, read-only DuckDB connection, 10-min wall-clock cap per job, ≤3 jobs per researcher run) |
| Session (D10) | one session per run, multi-turn genuinely helps here; discarded after | same |
| Output | `NightlyReview{lessons[], param_suggestions: [{parameter, proposed_value, evidence_refs}], process_errors[], trade_attributions[], summary}` → ledger + Telegram daily summary (`param_suggestions` feed §6.4 step 1, never applied directly) | `WeeklyResearch{strategy_assessments[], champ_chall_recommendations[], proposed_logic_changes[] (owner approval, R4), alias_theme_suggestions[] (§2.7 — owner approves, never auto-applied), action_items[]}` → dashboard report + owner action items |

### 5.6 Token-budget governor (D6) — concrete numbers for the $200/**week** subscription quota (D3, re-scoped 2026-09-12)

**Mechanism:** no credit-balance API exists (D6). The governor reads per-call usage (input/output/cache-read/cache-write) from SDK result messages, prices at standard rates from `config/agents.yaml` (D4 table), and appends to `budget_ledger` (SQLite). **The budget period is the subscription's WEEKLY quota window — Thursday 14:00 IST to the next Thursday 14:00 IST — not the calendar month (owner-directed 2026-09-12).** The SDK draws on that weekly quota, so a month-keyed ladder measured the wrong thing in both directions: a five-week month reads healthy while one week blows the quota, and a per-agent trip latches for the rest of the month (news_analyst on 08-28 and 09-09 held the intraday forward cap at 4/day for three weeks over spend that was comfortably inside the total). The window anchor is `llm.quota_window` (`reset_weekday: thursday`, `reset_time_ist: "14:00"`); the key is the start Thursday's ISO date and spend is a half-open `at` range over the ledger (index `idx_budget_at`, migration 0012). The reset is **wall-clock and calendar-blind** — a Thursday holiday still rolls the window; the NSE calendar shapes only the pace denominator. `llm.weekly_credit_usd: 200` is config, sized at ~2x the peak observed week (09-03: $103.13 total). The dollar is a governor PROXY — billing is the subscription quota, there is no console figure to reconcile (2026-08-13). Pay-as-you-go overflow default **off**; if the owner enables it, a hard monthly cap `llm.overflow_cap_usd: 25` applies (D6).

**Weekly allocations** [tunable] (**re-based 2026-09-12 from the retired monthly split**): Intraday Analyst **$90** · News Analyst **$90** · Pre-open Planner **$8** · Nightly Reviewer **$5** · Weekly Researcher **$2** · Reserve **$5** (sum $200). Sized at ~2x the peak observed week (09-03: intraday $42.78, news $54.58, preopen $3.66, nightly $1.98; the four prior weeks $45–76), inside the ~5x real bandwidth headroom the owner stated 2026-08-18. Per-agent allocation exhausted ⇒ that agent blocked for the rest of the WINDOW (reserve assignable by owner via `/budget`); at >85% of its own allocation the agent is `agent_degraded` — which throttles only **its own** cadence knobs, never the platform tier (see DG1). The catalyst digest itself is deterministic — $0.

**Degrade ladder (cache-aware per D8: shrink context and cut call classes before lengthening intervals or switching models — a model switch invalidates cache and can cost more than it saves):**

| Tier | Trip condition (whichever first) | LLM behavior | Mode capability mapping (D6) |
|---|---|---|---|
| DG0 | below the DG1 trip condition (≤110% pace) | full operation | AUTO may originate entries; RECOMMEND full |
| DG1 | >110% pro-rata **only** — the per-agent test was removed from the platform tier 2026-09-12 | heartbeat 20→45 min; context trimmed (drop bar history to 10 bars, drop sector block); pre-screen cap cut **proportionally** to `floor(base × degrade_ladder.DG1.degraded_forward_cap_frac)` = floor(48 × 0.67) = **32/day** (the old flat 4 was written when the base was 6 and became a 92% cut at base 48). The same two knobs also fire per-agent, off the platform tier: `agent_degraded('intraday_analyst')` slows the heartbeat and cuts the cap; `agent_degraded('news_analyst')` coarsens the news batch to 60 min | unchanged — analysis quality still "full" grade |
| DG2 | >125% pro-rata or window spend ≥85% of the credit ($170) | heartbeats off; signal + position events only; nightly reviewer context halved; weekly deferred if global >90%; news-analyst batching coarsens (60-min cadence, pre-open batch kept) | **AUTO stops opening new positions (manage-only) + owner notified** — below the defined full-analysis tier, entries are not LLM-originated (D6); RECOMMEND continues (marked DG2) |
| DG3 | window spend ≥95% of the credit ($190) | all intraday LLM calls off; news analyst off ⇒ **clusters unscored ⇒ empty catalyst watchlist ⇒ `cat` origination disabled (fails to zero, §2.7)** | AUTO manage-only persists (Tier 2/3 deterministic); RECOMMEND suspends LLM recommendations — dashboard/Telegram clearly switch to "scanner-only alerts (non-LLM)" |
| DG4 | window spend ≥ the credit ($200) / SDK billing errors | zero SDK calls until the next Thursday 14:00 IST rollover (the latch is keyed on the WINDOW, so the quota refilling clears it with no owner action) or owner enables overflow | identical to LLM-unavailable safe state (D6/D7); all R3 protection unaffected |

Governor state is published on `budget.state`, shown on the dashboard, and Telegram-alerted on every tier change (R8). Trading-calendar-aware (R6): no burn on holidays; pro-rata pace is computed over *trading sessions* inside the window, where the two boundary Thursdays count **0.5 each** when they are trading days (Thursday morning is billed to the window that ends at 14:00, the afternoon to the one that starts there). Elapsed includes today; an unmeasurable pace is returned as **`None`, never as 0** (a 0 is indistinguishable from "nothing should have been spent yet"), and the two pace rungs are skipped entirely on it — reached either when no calendar exists for the year or when no session has elapsed yet (a reset Thursday that is itself a holiday opens the window on a zero-session day), and comparing against it would turn ">125% of pace" into "any spend at all" and trip DG2 on the first cent of the week. The absolute ≥85%/≥95%/≥100% rungs still bind throughout. **Trade-window-aware (§1.4 item 11): the Intraday Analyst's entry-seeking triggers (signal candidates + heartbeat) fire only inside the owner-set window, so its §11.2 call count is a window-bounded ceiling — a 30–60 min window spends well under it. Its position-event trigger (§5.2 b, risk-reducing only) still fires whenever the engine is up. The News Analyst (market hours + 19:00–22:00 sweep + pre-open batch, §5.4) and the deterministic pre-screen scanners incl. `cat` (whenever the engine is up, feeding features/baselines — §3.2.5/§6.1) are NOT window-gated; only candidate-forwarding to the analyst is. The pre-open planner (08:50), catalyst digest (~08:35, deterministic), and nightly/weekly agents are unaffected.**

---

## 6. Strategy & learning design

### 6.1 Initial strategy set — rule-based baselines the LLM layer enhances

Four **price-only** deterministic baselines — each independently backtestable and paper-tradeable **without any LLM**, the non-LLM controls (output-spec §6 requirement) — plus the news-catalyst baseline `cat` (§2.7, added 2026-07-06), whose scanner is equally deterministic but consumes persisted LLM news scores through the watchlist seam (replayable without re-invoking the LLM, §9.6; the four price baselines remain the fully-LLM-free control group). The LLM's role on top of each: veto/confirm candidates with context the rules can't see (news, regime, correlated moves), pick among simultaneous candidates, and write the thesis. Attribution in the ledger separates "baseline alone" from "baseline + LLM filter" (shadow-recorded both ways); **for `cat`, baseline signals are recorded only on in-window confirmation bars — identical to the tradeable rule — so baseline-vs-LLM comparisons share one signal population.**

| ID | Style | Rule sketch | Why this baseline |
|---|---|---|---|
| `orb` | intraday (MIS) | Opening-range breakout: range = first `orb_minutes` after 09:15 (auction open per A14); enter on close beyond range with volume ≥ `vol_mult`×20-bar median (suppressed on `flagged_instrument_days`); stop = `stop_range_frac`×(entry − range low) for BUY (mirror: ×(range high − entry) for SELL) — anchored at the **opposite opening-range edge** (**v2 2026-07-12, owner-directed**: replaced `stop_atr_mult`×ATR(14,1m) — the 1m-ATR stop unit, ~0.14% of price median, sat below the cost floor: the ₹20k MIS round-trip ≈ 40–90% of the stop distance, making negative net expectancy structural; 2026-07-12 CPCV 0/15 folds at textbook zero-edge win rates); a candidate whose risk < 2× round-trip breakeven is not economically viable (C3) — enforced live by the §7.1 cost gate, modelled as an entry skip in Phase-1 sweeps; target = `rr_target`×risk; entries 09:30–14:30 **further intersected with the owner-set `trade_window`, and the position squared off by window end (§7.1 `trade_window`/`max_holding`)** | Classic, well-studied, cheaply computed; stop at the structural invalidation level (range far edge), not a noise-scale unit; the binding intraday constraint is now the trade window |
| `rsi2` | swing (CNC) | RSI(2) < `rsi_entry` on stocks above 200-DMA in an uptrending index; exit RSI(2) > `rsi_exit` or `max_hold_days`; stop = `stop_pct` gap-adjusted (R2) | Mean reversion with regime filter; T+1-friendly (C5) |
| `trend` | position (CNC) | 20/50 EMA cross with ADX > `adx_min`; trail `trail_atr_mult`×ATR(14,1d); max hold 120 td (R2). **Warm-up floor raised 60→150 completed dailies (WO-11, 2026-08-13, audit finding F10):** EMA(50)'s deterministic from-series-start seed carries a residual weight of `(1 − 2/51)^n` — 9.07% at 60 bars, still-material seed noise, vs 0.25% at 150 bars (the derivation is stated in `scanners/trend.py`'s module docstring); a young listing between 60 and 150 sessions old now emits no `trend` signal (previously it could, via `WarmupGate`'s young-listing exemption, carrying that seed noise into a live golden cross) | Slow, low-churn — DP-charge-friendly (C4) |
| `mom` | swing (CNC) | 4-week momentum rank within universe, top `top_n`, hold `rebalance_days`, ex-date aware (A12). **Rebalance-day state now live (WO-13, 2026-08-13, resolves the v1 gap noted at introduction, audit finding F11):** the platform persists the last-rebalanced trading session (`mom_rebalance_state`, one row, `engine.ops.scan_context`) and the live scanner fires only when `mom_sessions_since_rebalance ≥ rebalance_days` — matching the sweep's fixed `valid_positions[::rebalance_days]` cadence — instead of the previous hard-coded `None` that made every live day read as rebalance-due; the marker survives an engine restart | Cross-sectional control; few, larger positions (C4) |
| `cat` | swing (CNC), **long-only** until the §1.4.9 short gate | **News-catalyst drift (O11/O12/O13, §2.7 — added 2026-07-06).** Eligibility: symbol on today's `catalyst_watchlist` at grade `originating` with direction long and event age ≤ `cat.max_event_age_days`. **Entry only on price/volume confirmation** — an in-window 1-minute bar close ≥ max(prior_close × (1 + `cat.confirm_move_pct`/100), high of the day's first 30 minutes — a fixed 30-min range, deliberately independent of the `orb` envelope) with relative volume ≥ `cat.confirm_vol_mult` × 20-bar median (suppressed on `flagged_instrument_days`). Stop = entry − `cat.stop_atr_mult` × ATR(14,1d); target = entry + `cat.rr_target` × (entry − stop); time-exit at `cat.max_hold_days` td. **Earnings events (O13):** day-T entry is banned by `no_trade_windows.no_entry_on_results_day`; the symbol becomes `cat`-eligible from **T+1**, requiring agreement = sign(qualifying earnings cluster's sentiment) == sign(close_T − open_T) from `bars_1d`; close_T == open_T ⇒ reaction sign 0 ⇒ NOT eligible (conservative) — classic PEAD. **Warm-up:** the 30-min range exists only once the 09:15–09:45 bars do (live or gap-backfilled, §2.6 — `warmup_ready` covers this lookback for `cat` too); no `cat` confirmation can trigger before 09:45 or with any of those bars missing | The only baseline that consumes news — through exactly **one seam** (the watchlist, §2.7); targets post-event drift, which fits the platform's 5–15 min news latency (O12); validation is forward-first (§2.7/§8.6) |

**`brk20` addendum (2026-08-04, owner-directed after the BPCL 2026-08-03 miss):** a sixth deterministic origination rule — 20-day-high **daily-close** breakout over the **full ELIGIBLE universe** (the watchlist cap makes sub-cap symbols structurally invisible to the per-bar scanners; BPCL sat at liquidity rank 96 and had never been watched). Long-only swing (CNC — cash equities cannot short overnight); fresh-cross only (the prior session must not already be above its own band); volume-confirmed (`vol_mult`×20d-mean — BPCL's 0.80×-volume breakout would still be REFUSED by default, deliberately); A12 ex-date skip; levels per the LIMIT-AT-LEVEL amendment below; §6.3 envelope `{lookback_days, vol_mult, rr_target, ex_skip_days}`. Architecturally NOT a per-bar `Scanner`: a pure batch rule (`scanners/brk20.py`) run by the window-open/`/scan_now` sweep and rendered as a §5.3 planner context section; candidates are admitted through `SignalPreScreen.admit` so §3.2.5 dedupe/caps bind identically (no cap bypass). Evidence caveat: momentum entries repeatedly tested net-negative at retail costs — `brk20` originates for Tier-1 judgement and owner decision; it carries no expectancy presumption. The same change raised `data.universe_max_watchlist` 50→100.

**`brk20` levels — LIMIT-AT-LEVEL (amended 2026-08-13, WO-4 / audit finding F5; supersedes the original `entry = close(y)`, `stop = H20` sketch).** The candidate's `raw_levels.entry` is the **broken level `H20` itself**, never yesterday's close. `close(y)` is a yesterday-print nobody can transact at; carrying it as the entry made the analyst's proposal — and the gate's LIMIT sizing — compute risk from a price better than obtainable (F5), and the owner payload rendered it verbatim. The trade plan keeps the rule's **own** risk unit `R = close(y) − H20` (the breakout extension — exactly the distance the original sketch used as `entry − stop`) and is **translated down onto the level**, so the geometry is unchanged and only the anchor moves: `entry = H20`; `stop = H20 − R = 2·H20 − close(y)` (the breakout has failed by as much as it succeeded); `target = entry + rr_target × (entry − stop)`. Reward:risk, the risk unit, and the score are all identical to the pre-amendment plan; the score stays keyed to `close(y)/H20` because breakout **margin** is the strength signal and is independent of where the plan is anchored. Rounding order is pinned — `entry`/`stop` tick-rounded first, `target` derived from the rounded pair — so the shipped levels satisfy the §7.1 `levels_coherent` shape (`stop < entry < target`) *after* rounding, not merely before it; a margin that vanishes under tick rounding leaves `entry <= stop` and emits nothing. Semantically the entry is now a **retest of the broken level**, which is what "limit-at-level" means and what the §3.6 payload states. Consequence, accepted deliberately: a breakout whose level sits further from the live price than the §7.1 `entry_sanity_band` (MIS 1% / CNC 2%) is now **hard-rejected** rather than sized off a stale close — an unfillable recommendation is worse than none, and the band's job is exactly to say so.

**`brk20` stop-geometry floor (2026-08-19, owner-directed — WO-19; the IDEA/LENSKART degeneracy).** The LIMIT-AT-LEVEL translation keeps the rule's own risk unit `R = close(y) − H20`, and a MARGINAL breakout therefore ships a *tiny* one: live occurrences IDEA 2026-08-17 (R = ₹0.10, two ticks) and 2026-08-19 — MFSL 0.61%, MCX 0.54%, LENSKART **0.17%** of entry, three of the day's four brk20 candidates. A swing stop thinner than the symbol's routine overnight gap is noise-level: it is stopped with high probability at the very next open while paying the full round-trip cost, and §7.1 sizing INVERTS on it (notional ≈ risk_budget ÷ (2.5 × stop%) — the thinnest stops request the largest notionals). The §7.1 `entry_sanity_band` is complementary, not a fix: it rejects unfillable ENTRIES at evaluation time (that is what actually caught LENSKART, price having run away), never an unsurvivable STOP shipped onto a quiet tape. **Rule, pinned:** per symbol compute `gaps = |open_t/close_{t−1} − 1|` over the last `gap_lookback_sessions` (20) completed session-pairs ending at `y`, skipping pairs with non-finite or ≤0 members; require ≥ `gap_min_sessions` (10) valid gaps — fewer ⇒ **no candidate** (`floor_unavailable`: a symbol whose overnight behaviour cannot be established does not ship a multi-night plan; conservative young-listing posture). Floor = `gap_floor_mult` (**2.0**) × median(gaps) × entry; the tick-exact `R` must be ≥ the floor (equality passes); below ⇒ **no candidate** (`gap_floor` veto). VETO, never widen: widening would silently re-shape the geometry the rule was pinned with and convert a marginal breakout into a different trade — §3.2.5 fail-to-zero. Rationale for 2×median: one *typical* night must not be able to consume more than half the risk unit (≈p75–p85 of |gap| under fat-tailed gap distributions); a design constant, deliberately NOT fitted. **Governance:** the floor knobs live in a module mapping SEPARATE from the §6.3 envelope dict (`{gap_floor_mult, gap_lookback_sessions, gap_min_sessions}` — owner-only, **never learnable**: a learner shrinking the floor re-opens the degeneracy; keeping them out of the envelope's dict makes that structural, not conventional). **Observability:** the sweep's call sites log per run `symbols_scanned / candidates / gap_floor_vetoes / floor_unavailable` — a silent veto class is undiagnosable (the `cat` visibility discipline). Mechanically, `DailyRow` gains a required `open` (bars_1d carries it); both call sites — the window-open sweep and the §5.3 planner context — construct it, and the required field makes a forgotten constructor fail loudly, never silently veto. `brk20` still carries no expectancy presumption; the floor tightens origination hygiene, not edge claims.

**`brk20` ENTRY-MECHANISM BACKTEST — PRE-REGISTERED 2026-09-12 (R1; written BEFORE the run, evidence only, wires nothing).** `brk20` has never been backtested at all, and its LIMIT-AT-LEVEL entry (the amendment two paragraphs up) was a 2026-08-13 fix for a sizing/render defect (WO-4 / audit F5), not an evidence-based choice of entry MECHANISM. The 2026-09-11 audit verification measured the live cost: of 15 `brk20` proposals, **6 died on the §7.1 `entry_sanity_band`** (the rested level >2% below LTP — IDEA 5.24%, PAYTM 4.01%, SAIL 2.88%, KOTAKBANK 4.65%, SYRMA 3.96%, CHENNPETRO 6.30%), the band being the SOLE reject cause for 3 of them, and those 3 are the top-3 `brk20` scores — score is monotone in breakout margin, so the mechanism systematically discards its own strongest signals. An external reviewer proposes entering AT MARKET on confirmation instead; neither mechanism has evidence for 20-session highs. This registration decides it by evidence and changes no code either way. **Population:** the ELIGIBLE universe — the CURRENT (latest `universe_daily` day) `included` rows plus `exclusion_reasons == ['watchlist_cap']` rows, i.e. exactly the set `store.get_universe_eligible_symbols` hands the live sweep — applied BACKWARDS over the whole window. That is a **survivorship-tainted proxy in the same sense the `hi52` report's index split is**: NIFTY 500 membership as of each signal date is stored nowhere in this platform, so a name that entered the index after a big run reads as eligible throughout that run and a dropped name is mislabelled the other way; every rendering carries the label and the JSON carries `population_is_survivorship_tainted_proxy: true`. The bars-level caveat rides along unchanged (`bars_1d` carries since-delisted history after the 2026-09-03 archive backfill, but a name delisted inside the horizon books no trade, so the residual bias is optimistic and only partly corrected). **Window:** the full `bars_1d` history. **Signal:** `brk20.scan_daily` is IMPORTED and called — the `H20` arithmetic, the fresh-cross test, the `vol_mult` 1.2 volume confirmation, the A12 ex-date skip and the WO-19 stop-geometry floor are never re-implemented — plus the live sweep's 35-calendar-day unadjusted-history veto over `corp_actions`. **Entry variants, THREE, fixed here:** **V1** = the next session's OPEN after the fresh cross (market-on-confirmation — the reviewer's proposal, and `ins`'s validated fill). **V2-3** / **V2-5** = the SHIPPED mechanism, a resting limit at the candidate's own `raw_levels.entry` (`round_to_tick(H20)`), filled on the FIRST session in `y+1 … y+N` whose low ≤ that level (N = 3, N = 5), at `min(open, level)` — a session that gaps through fills at its open, never at a price the tape did not print; unfilled at N ⇒ **no trade**, and the FILL RATE is a first-class reported number because an unfilled breakout is the mechanism's real cost. **Trial count N = 3** (three variants of one family), `fold_pass_min(3)` = 60%; nothing else is swept and no "best of" is selected anywhere. **Horizons:** T+5 / T+10 / T+20 trading sessions FROM THE FILL, exiting at the close of session `fill + k`. Deliberate divergence from the `hi52`/`event_study` signal-anchored convention, stated here so it cannot be read as drift: the variants fill on DIFFERENT sessions, so a signal anchor would charge V2 for the days it spent waiting; the fill anchor measures the mechanism, which is the question asked. All three variants share ONE **signal** population — a signal is admitted only when a full `y + 5 + 20` of forward bars exists — but they do **NOT** share a TRADE set, and the pre-run phrasing that said the variants are “quoted on the same event set” is STRUCK (corrected 2026-09-12 after the run audit): a V2 limit fills only when price RETURNS to the broken level, i.e. conditional on the breakout not running away, so each V2 variant trades a pullback-SELECTED subset (8,292 / 4,230 / 4,898 trades on the run below) and every pooled V1-vs-V2 number is a comparison PER FILLED TRADE across two different event sets. The MATCHED-COHORT DECOMPOSITION in the RUN record is what makes the two mechanisms comparable. **Costs:** one full CNC round trip, both legs, from `CostModel.breakeven_pct(₹20,000, "CNC")` = **0.3192%** (0.2992% statutory fees + 0.0200% measured spread, WO-2) — the same floor the `hi52` harness charges. **Sizing:** per-trade equal notional, percent returns, event study; no compounding, no portfolio construction, no concurrency cap. **Metrics per variant:** n, fill rate, median/mean gross and net, hit rate, the ORB-lesson geometry line (median GROSS vs the cost floor) printed BEFORE any signal-quality claim, and CPCV through `engine.learning.validate.cpcv_splits` with purge = embargo = the horizon and the series keyed on the FILL date, fed to the §6.4 `promotion_decision` at N=3 with the WO-3 margin floor. **Mandatory split: breakout-margin TERCILE** (`close(y)/H20 − 1`), because the live rejections concentrate in the top tercile — a wide margin is exactly what puts the rested level far below LTP. The tercile cuts are full-sample statistics of the measured population and are therefore DESCRIPTIVE, not a tradeable filter (the `hi52` smooth/jumpy caveat, verbatim in force). **Promotion rule:** the house §6.4 rule, unchanged. **REPORTING RULE — AMENDMENT 2026-09-12 (added after the run audit; a TIGHTENING of how a result is REPORTED, NOT a new selection, NOT a new trial and NOT a re-registration):** a variant × horizon is reported **PROMOTABLE only if the CPCV/deflation rule passes AND the geometry is viable at that horizon (median NET > 0** — the TYPICAL trade clears one full round trip). The two gates are built on different statistics and were free to disagree silently: the CPCV fold series is a per-fill-day MEAN net (house convention, `scripts/backtest_hi52.py:843`) while every geometry and decision statistic is a MEDIAN, so a tail-driven cell can read “promotable” while its median trade pays the round trip for nothing — which is exactly V1 at T+10 in the run below (CPCV fold pass 60.0%, median net −0.3192%): that cell now reads **NOT viable, NOT promotable**. The amendment can only WITHHOLD a label, never grant one, so nothing the registered rule refused can be promoted by it; the registered decision rule is evaluated and reported under BOTH gates on every run (`decision` and `decision_under_tightened_reporting_rule` in the JSON) and the report states whether the tightening changes the outcome. **DECISION RULE for the entry mechanism, fixed before the run:** prefer the variant with the higher median NET return at **T+10**, PROVIDED that variant is CPCV-promotable at T+10; if no variant is CPCV-promotable at T+10 the outcome is **`neither`**, the shipped mechanism stands unchanged, and the study is entitled to say only that it found no evidence to move it. A variant winning this comparison is evidence for a mechanism change, never a promotion of `brk20` itself: the rule continues to carry no expectancy presumption, and any wiring is a separate §8.6 owner decision. **RUN 2026-09-12 01:22, CORRECTED RE-RUN 2026-09-12 12:02 after the audit (same data, every number below reproduced to 4 dp; the re-run adds the matched-cohort cells, the per-cell fill rates and the tightened reporting gate) (engine off, `scripts/backtest_brk20.py`, window 2022-08-01→2026-08-07 signal dates over the full stored `bars_1d`, 480 eligible symbols, 1,042 sessions; 8,461 fresh crosses fired, 8,292 admitted; vetoes `gap_floor` 3,872 / `floor_unavailable` 0 / `unadjusted_history` 20; `data/reports/backtest_brk20_2026-09-12.json` and `.md`):** V1 (market-on-confirmation) fill rate 100%, median net **−0.5636% / −0.3192% / +0.6319%** at T+5/T+10/T+20, geometry **dead at T+5 and T+10**, CPCV-gate pass at T+10 (fold **60.0%** — boundary-exact, one fold from failing) and T+20 (80.0%), but REPORTED promotable only at **T+20**: at T+10 the geometry is dead, so the 2026-09-12 amendment withholds the label. V2-3 fill rate **51.0%**, median net −0.3783% / −0.0972% / +0.9143%, geometry dead at T+5/T+10, CPCV-gate pass at T+10 (73.3%) / T+20 (80.0%), REPORTED promotable only at **T+20**. V2-5 fill rate **59.1%**, median net −0.3025% / **+0.0513%** / **+0.9168%**, the ONLY variant geometry-viable at T+10 (margin +0.05% over the floor), CPCV-gate pass and REPORTED promotable at **T+10** (73.3%) and **T+20** (80.0%). **DECISION-RULE OUTCOME, verbatim: `V2_limit_at_H20_N5`** — the shipped LIMIT-AT-LEVEL anchor stands and the reviewer's market-on-confirmation proposal does not win the registered rule. Under the 2026-09-12 tightened reporting gate the outcome is `V2_limit_at_H20_N5` as well (V1 and V2-3 are withheld at T+10 as NOT viable; V2-5 is the only reported-promotable cell there), so **the tightening does not change the registered outcome**. Reported-promotable cells (CPCV *and* geometry): V1 at T+20 only, V2-3 at T+20 only, V2-5 at T+10 and T+20. **The earlier “REFUTED … at every horizon and on every cell” reading is WITHDRAWN**: it compared different trade sets. **MATCHED-COHORT DECOMPOSITION (2026-09-12, from the same trade objects — a DESCRIPTIVE decomposition: no new variant, no new parameter, no new selection, not a fourth trial):** V1 re-quoted on exactly the cohort each V2 variant FILLED and on exactly the cohort it never filled, which separates the entry PRICE effect from the SELECTION effect. V2-5 at T+10 — V1 on the FILLED cohort (n=4,898) median net **−2.2347%** against V2-5's **+0.0513%** → **PRICE effect +2.2860 pp**; V1 on the UNFILLED cohort (n=3,394) median net **+2.2234%** (T+20 **+3.5293%**, T+5 +1.9573%) → **SELECTION effect −4.4581 pp**. V2-3 has the same shape (T+10: price +2.0084 pp on n=4,230 filled, selection −3.6352 pp on n=4,062 unfilled), and the signs hold at all three horizons. **Read plainly: V2's advantage is carried ENTIRELY by the entry PRICE — on the SAME signals the broken level is a materially better fill than the next open — while the SELECTION runs hard AGAINST the limit, because the ~41% of breakouts that never come back are the best cohort V1 books (median net +3.53% at T+20) and the resting limit forfeits them.** That is why the pooled T+10 gap is only +0.37 pp while the matched price effect is +2.29 pp. This study measures per FILLED TRADE and does NOT price a deployment per SIGNAL ORIGINATED; the forfeited-cohort number above is the one the retest-re-arm question turns on. **MARGIN TERCILE, per cohort with n per cell:** on the MATCHED cohort V2-5 beats V1 in all three terciles at all three horizons — narrowest 2,764 signals / 2,149 filled (fill rate 77.8%), price effect +1.2450 pp at T+10; middle 2,764 / 1,685 (61.0%), +2.3688 pp; widest 2,764 / 1,064 (38.5%), +5.0540 pp — so the MATCHED claim holds, with those n. The earlier UNMATCHED “in every margin tercile” claim is WITHDRAWN: unmatched tercile rows of two variants cover different signals, and every cell in the report now prints its own fill rate. The widest tercile is simultaneously where the limit fills LEAST (38.5%) and where its matched price advantage is LARGEST, and V2-5's best cell (T+20 median net **+1.1340%**, n=1,064) is exactly the cohort the live §7.1 `entry_sanity_band` rejects — an origination-TIMING argument (the retest re-arm), never an argument for widening the band; the plan's reasoning on the band is untouched. **Honest reading, not softened:** the edge is a **T+20** edge for all three variants (every one is geometry-dead at T+5; only V2-5 clears the floor at T+10 and by 0.05%, which sits inside the optimism band of the touch-fill assumption — a daily low ≤ level counts as a fill here and `min(open, level)` books the open on a gap-through, so the V2 fill rates are an UPPER bound and both divergences flatter V2); V1's median GROSS at T+10 **rounds to +0.0000% at 4 dp** (the stored statistic is rounded, so it is not provably exactly zero), i.e. its median trade pays about a full round trip for nothing; and the live mechanism as shipped publishes the level ONCE with no retest re-arm, so a single-session resting limit (N=1) was not among the registered variants and has NO measured return here — its fill rate is readable from the registered delay histogram at **31.4%** (2,601 of 8,292), and a return for it needs its own pre-registration. Population caveat unchanged and material: survivorship-tainted proxy, optimistic on a continuation study. Nothing is wired: `brk20` still carries no expectancy presumption and the retest re-arm remains an owner decision.

**`brk20` RETEST RE-ARM — WIRED 2026-09-12 (WO-R; the mechanism the backtest above selected, and nothing more).** The study one paragraph up returned `V2_limit_at_H20_N5` under its own pre-registered decision rule, and the live rule did not implement it: `brk20` publishes the broken level **once**, on the crossing session, under the §3.2.5 same-day `(symbol, strategy)` dedupe — i.e. it shipped **N=1**, a variant that was never registered, has **no measured return**, and whose fill rate reads **31.4%** off the registered delay histogram against V2-5's **59.1%**. **MECHANISM:** every `brk20` candidate the sweep **ADMITS** (admitted, not raw — the pre-screen has already decided which levels are worth judgement) is journalled into `brk20_resting_levels` (migration 0014) with its own `raw_levels`, its own score and an `expires_d` = the **5th TRADING session** after the crossing session, resolved through `NSECalendar.next_trading_day` (a calendar-blind `+5 days` would shorten the window across every long weekend). On each session in `(signal_d, expires_d]`, the 60 s `forward_drain_tick` — gated on the sweep's own `_sweep_window_active` predicate, so a live session inside the owner trade window in a mode that originates, and nothing at all outside it — expires stale rows and re-offers each resting level **the first minute its live price is back inside the §7.1 `entry_sanity_band`** (`abs(entry − ltp)/ltp × 100 ≤ cnc_pct`, the gate's formula and its `≤` edge verbatim, read at the enforcement site from the hash-verified limits table). `owner knob: settings.yaml brk20.retest_sessions: 5` — owner-only and **never learnable**, because the number *is* the registered mechanism (N=1 and N=3 were both measured and both lost; every other N was never run). The window is `5`, not "until it fills", and state is **SQLite, not process memory**: the 2026-09-11 forensics recorded the in-memory forward queue being lost on every mid-session reboot, and a multi-session mechanism whose state does not survive a restart silently degenerates back into the single-session one it replaces. **RELATION TO THE D1 (e) BAND SCREEN (`pipeline.BAND_SKIP_STRATEGIES`, 2026-09-11):** complements, not duplicates. That screen **DEFERS within a day** — it keeps a queued `brk20` candidate out of a scarce analyst slot while its level sits outside the band, and it can only act on a candidate still in today's forward queue, which empties at its TTL; this **re-arms across days**, from durable state, for a level whose candidate is long gone. Both read the same band from the same table; neither widens it. **WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO, stated because the temptation runs the other way:** (i) **no new entry price** — the re-publication carries the crossing session's `raw_levels` and score verbatim, so a retest is the same trade offered again, never a re-anchored one; (ii) **no widened band** — the band condition is a NARROWING (a level outside it is not offered at all), and nothing here can make a candidate the gate would reject acceptable; (iii) **no new admission capacity** — every re-publication goes through the SAME `prescreen.admit` (day cap, per-strategy cap, same-day dedupe) and the same §7.1 gate, so a retest competes for the same slot as a fresh candidate and wins nothing by being one, and the once-a-day offer bound keeps the minute cadence out of the WO-9 raw counters; (iv) **no expectancy claim** — the study chose a MECHANISM between two mechanisms on a survivorship-tainted eligible-universe proxy; it promoted no rule, `brk20` stays **exploratory** with no expectancy presumption, and its analyst contract says so alongside the one sentence that tells the analyst an older breakout date is the mechanism working rather than a stale signal; (v) **no wider population and no weaker trigger** — see the four extra narrowings below, each of which can only REFUSE a re-offer. **THE FOUR NARROWINGS ON TOP OF THE BAND (2026-09-12 review), all fail-to-zero:** (a) **a FRESH price** — `ltp_fn` is the tick cache and carries no age, so the re-offer also reads `main.tick_age_s` and refuses anything older than the §7.1 `stale_data_guard.max_tick_age_s` the gate will judge it by (read from the SAME limits load as the band, so the two can never straddle an owner edit). Everywhere else an unaged LTP only DEFERS (the D1 (e) screen) or fails closed at the gate; this is the first place it would SPEND a day slot, and the exposure is worst for exactly the sub-cap names the feed subscription was added to reach. (b) **ONE trade per level** — a symbol already HELD, or carrying an unexpired unactioned entry recommendation, is not re-offered: that is the mechanism the backtest measured (V2-5 books one trade per signal), and `gate._rule_per_stock_exposure` would reject it as `already held or pending` — a HARD reason no shrink can cure — only AFTER the §3.2.5 slot and one of the day's (DG1-capped) analyst calls were spent. (c) **the A12 ex-date veto, re-applied** — `bars_1d` is never re-adjusted across an ex-date, so a level journalled before one is a pre-ex price the tape stops quoting; `brk20.scan_daily` refuses such a symbol on the crossing day and the re-offer refuses it on the retest day, over brk20's own `ex_skip_days` horizon (the retest window is 5 trading sessions = 7–11 calendar days, so the two genuinely overlap). (d) **the registered population** — a symbol that has left the ELIGIBLE universe since the crossing stops being re-offered; `brk20`'s origination scope IS that population and a journalled level may not outlive it. (c) and (d) both read the sets **today's sweep already computed**, so the minute pulse pays nothing for them. **ORDERING AND PLACEMENT:** the tick is armed for a day only once that day's **in-window sweep has published**, because the pre-screen's `(symbol, strategy)` dedupe is first-come — a level broken days ago landing ahead of the day's ranked admission would take the slot, today's fresh crossing of the same symbol would be dropped as a duplicate and, being un-admitted, never journalled either, leaving the book on the stale geometry for the rest of the window. The whole body runs through `asyncio.to_thread`, as `prescreen.handle_bar` and the sweep's own `_collect_and_scan` do: it takes `SignalPreScreen._lock` and then mints a feature snapshot under `MarketStore._lock`, which a partition COPY holds for ~59 s and the 2026-08-21 stall held for ~14 minutes — inline, that would freeze the event loop, i.e. the tick cache, the §2.2 heartbeat, the kill path and every scheduled job. The journal WRITE likewise runs on the loop thread beside `_consume_ins_pending` (single writer, §4.1): the connection is `isolation_level=None`, so a bare INSERT from the sweep worker can join a `core.db.transaction` the loop is holding and vanish with its ROLLBACK — silently, after `record` has already logged the level as resting. **TELEMETRY:** `brk20_retest_offered` (the book, per level handed to the pre-screen) and `brk20_retest_republished` (the wiring, per level `prescreen.admit` ACCEPTED) are separate events, so neither stage of the WO-9 funnel is read off the other; the per-symbol refusal lines are bounded to one a day each. Fail-to-zero throughout (§3.2.5/D7): an unreadable journal, an unparseable row, a raising `ltp_fn`, a limits store that will not verify, an unevaluable not-offerable screen, or a calendar past its verified horizon each cost at most a single re-offer — never the drain tick, never the sweep.

**`orb` parked (2026-09-12, owner-directed after the 09-11 verification).** `strategy.prescreen.max_per_strategy_day.orb: 0` — a per-strategy cap of 0 now means "admit nothing for this strategy" (refused before any slot is charged, counted under `suppressed_disabled`, logged once a day; `default: 0` stays an error). Evidence: since the forwarded counter existed (08-14) orb took 101 of 229 analyst forwards and produced ZERO entry proposals — every entry proposal came from a swing leg — and its 20-minute TTL against the 3-minute pacing re-armed and re-published the same pair repeatedly; under the degraded forward cap that waste became decisive. Three intraday price-only families are refuted at retail cost (ORB 0/15 CPCV twice, `tdc` and the candle battery on 09-04; VWAP-deviation reversion aborted at stage 1 under WO-10b). Re-enabling any intraday origination is gated on a pre-registered hypothesis that clears the cost floor (§8.6); the two untested candidates recorded on 09-11 are an ATR- or beta-conditioned population (as a split on the existing harnesses) and the short/fade side, which no intraday backtest has ever run (every sweep was long-only under §1.4.9; the ORB v2 negative-gross fade in 2025-H2 is the prior). Two silent starvations became visible the same day: `rsi2` logs `rsi2_regime_blocked` once per session when the NIFTY 50 index-regime leg fails (it has failed every session since 08-27 — rsi2's silence since 08-28 is the regime filter working, not a defect; the stock leg alone would pass 13–32 names a day), and the `ins` crossings job logs `ins_feed_coverage_low` when issuers-with-filings fall under 10% of the eligible universe (09-10: 22 of 480 — the live BSE feed covers 22 issuers while the validated NSE corpus had 1,565; restoring coverage is the highest-value origination task and an owner decision on sourcing).

**`ins` addendum (2026-08-17, owner-directed — the first evidence-first origination leg).** A seventh deterministic rule, promoted from the §2.8.4 filings evidence because it is the **only recorded edge that survived the corrected-mechanics re-check** (WO-16, 2026-08-14: T+10 **+0.7297%** / T+20 **+1.5797%** net at next-open fills + full spread-inclusive costs; CPCV +0.0359%/day with the median passing split 4× the WO-3 margin floor → promotable; standing caveats restated below, not hidden). **Rule:** a symbol's trailing-10-session open-market insider net-BUY value crosses ≥ `ins.threshold_inr` (₹1,00,00,000 — **owner-fixed, NOT learnable**: the threshold defines the validated event population; moving it re-opens multiplicity), anchored on the **disclosure broadcast timestamp** (never the transaction date), with the §2.8.4 re-arm hysteresis — and the live code MUST consume the same crossing function the validated study runs (one definition; a copy would drift). Long-only swing (CNC), **batch over the full eligible universe** (brk20's architectural shape — filings arrive EOD; no per-bar scanning). **Timing:** a date-keyed EOD job (`ins_crossings`, after the filings-fresh ingest) computes the day's NEW crossings and persists pending candidates; the next session's window-open sweep admits them through `SignalPreScreen.admit` (§3.2.5 caps/dedupe bind identically; per-strategy cap `ins`). **Levels — the live translation, pre-registered:** entry = **next-session open reference** (the validated fill; the WO-16 close_t A/B showed the edge is slow — minutes of open-slippage are immaterial); `stop = entry × (1 − ins.stop_pct/100)`, default **5%** — a **disaster stop, deliberately a deviation from the stopless validated design**, required because §7.1 sizing needs a risk distance; as wide as the gate's economics permit (see the corrected arithmetic) so the T+10/T+20 drift capture is rarely interrupted (its interference is measurable in the ledger and reviewable); `target = None` (payload states the validated T+20 median +1.21% as information, never an order level); **exit = TIME: hold to the §7.1 `max_holding` swing cap of 20 trading days — exactly the validated T+20 horizon — so the existing max-holding machinery IS the exit path** (no new exit code; the T+10 variant remains recorded evidence, not a rule). Expected-edge input to the C3 cost gate = the validated T+20 net (+1.58%). **Sizing arithmetic — CORRECTED same-day (2026-08-17): the original 6%-stop draft omitted §7.1 `per_trade_risk.overnight_gap_mult` (2.5×), which charges every swing unit 2.5× its stop distance.** With it: unit risk = 2.5 × stop%, qty = ₹400 ÷ (2.5 × stop% × price), so notional ≈ ₹400/(2.5 × stop%) — ₹4,000 at 4%, ₹3,200 at 5%, ₹2,667 at 6% — and the DP flat charge grows as notional shrinks, so a WIDER stop makes the economics WORSE, not safer. Measured edge multiples against the shipped cost surface: 4% → 2.52× PASS, 5% → 2.19× PASS, **6% → 1.90× REJECT**, 7–8% → reject. The `[4–8]` future-envelope range is therefore annotated: only **[4–5] is economically viable** at the current capital/cost structure — the binding constraint is the gap-multiplier × DP-charge interaction, not the edge. Default set to 5% (the widest viable point). The gate limits themselves were NOT touched — widening `edge_multiple_min` or `overnight_gap_mult` to admit a strategy is precisely the move the governance forbids. **Live-reachability (WO-16 caveat, binding):** live crossings are computable only from the **BSE fresh feed** (live since 2026-07-19, ~13–18 in-universe rows/day); the NSE PIT feed's ~70-day content embargo makes it historical-only — so the live-reachable event population is NOT proven identical to the backtested one. The `ins_crossings` job logs fresh-feed row counts and crossings found every run; sustained zero-rows is starvation, not absence of signal. **Governance:** ships RECOMMEND-only (Phase 2 is structurally so); Phase-4 live-AUTO enablement follows the `cat` precedent (owner approval as new strategy logic + soak evidence, §8.6). `ins.stop_pct` [4–8] and `ins.hold_sessions` [10–20] are future envelope rows — **static owner knobs in `settings.yaml` for Phase 2** (no protected-store churn until the learning phase needs them). Remaining honest caveats carried from WO-16: the CPCV pass is boundary-exact (60.0% vs a 60% bar — one fold from failure), and the survivorship/index-membership bound is uncorrectable with stored data — `ins` is the best-evidenced leg, not a proven money-printer.

**`hi52` addendum (2026-09-01, owner-directed — the JINDALSAW/BALRAMCHIN + 13-movers review). ⚠ SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-09-12 by the `hi52` v2 PROMOTION addendum below — the shadow registration, the `get_batch_universe_symbols` population and the "diagnostics never gate" clause are all retired; what still stands here is the origin evidence, the pinned v1 trigger arithmetic and the v2 pre-registration.** An eighth deterministic rule, born as a **SHADOW** (registered in the gate's `no_edge_shadow_strategies` at birth — C3 rejects every candidate unconditionally; ADMISSION is the validation population, exactly the `cat` mechanics). **Origin evidence:** on 2026-09-01 all six owner-flagged out-of-universe movers (ENGINERSIN +7.2%, SSWL +14.9%, DYCL +10.1%, CAPLIPOINT +8.1%, WELCORP +6.9%, AEROFLEX +5.4%) printed or approached fresh 52-week/all-time highs, four already within ~2–7% of their prior high at the previous close — while all six flagged NIFTY200 movers sat 62–87% of their highs (no breakout shape). The platform had NO 52wk-high construct anywhere (verified absence, 2026-09-01 audit) and the movers' class sat outside the index universe entirely (§3.2.4 extended-leg addendum, shipped same day). **Rule:** long-only swing; fire iff `close(y)/max(high, 252 sessions incl. y) ≥ proximity_min` (0.95) as a **fresh cross** (previous session's ratio below the threshold — a stock parked above the band never re-fires), volume(y) ≥ `vol_mult` × 20-session mean, ex-date skip 10 sessions; entry ref = close(y), stop 6% (disaster stop), `target = None`; batch over `get_batch_universe_symbols` (the ONLY strategy on the extended set — deliberately safe because shadow); per-strategy prescreen cap 3. Path-smoothness diagnostics (up-day fraction, max single-day move over 20 sessions) journal alongside for the Frog-in-the-Pan analysis, never gate. **Evidence base (2026-09-01 research memo, adversarially verified):** the 52wk-high effect is a weeks-to-months drift (George & Hwang 2004 JoF; India: Raju 2023 SSRN; Della Vedova et al. 2023 JFQA; ~0.4–0.9%/mo L-S), NOT an intraday effect — per-day share 1–4bps vs ~30–70bps realistic intraday round-trip, the same cost-geometry that killed ORB 0/15 twice; Frog-in-the-Pan (Da/Gurun/Warachka 2014 RFS: continuous-info momentum +5.94% vs discrete/news-driven −2.07%) retroactively explains the 3× cat-confirmation refutation and motivates the quiet-approach preference. Internal negatives cover intraday through T+5; T+5..T+20 is untested and the `ins` leg proves that horizon clears costs here. **Pre-registered backtest protocol (the §8.6 owner-gate input; run on bars_1d full-market bhavcopy history, engine-idle):** continuous proximity RANK across the batch universe (the academic construct — not only the discrete trigger), entry next-open after the fresh cross, horizons T+5/T+10/T+20, full spread-inclusive costs both legs, CPCV with the WO-3 margin floor, deflated metrics, mandatory splits: smooth vs jumpy approaches (Frog-in-the-Pan), news-gap-day exclusion A/B, index members vs extended names, and the ORB-lesson arithmetic check (edge vs cost floor) BEFORE any signal-quality claim. Promotion to a ninth actionable rule needs the backtest + shadow soak + owner sign-off (§8.6); until then zero RECOMMENDs by construction. **Unadjusted-history veto (2026-09-03):** no `bars_1d` source re-adjusts STORED history across an ex-date (Kite candles are adjusted at fetch time, A11, but the series is seeded once and extended one session a day; bhavcopy rows are raw), so a symbol with a bonus/split/rights/demerger ex-date inside the 252-session window holds bars in two units — the live sweep skips it (`hi52.unadjusted_history` over `corp_actions` for the frame window, counted `unadjusted_history`) and the backtest must apply the same veto over the same table. The same premise holds for `brk20`'s 20-session high, so from 2026-09-09 its sweep applies the same veto over a 35-calendar-day trailing window (counted `unadjusted_vetoes` on the `brk20_sweep` line). Precondition: `corp_actions` carries windowed rows only since 2026-08-14 — `scripts/backfill_bhavcopy.py --skip-bhavcopy` over the lookback (and its bars leg for the 2022→2026 population) fills it, engine off. **Full-market re-run (2026-09-03, after the archive backfill: 3,199 symbols, 16,042 trades, veto applied):** the fresh-cross edge is an INDEX-CLASS effect — index proxy T+20 median net +1.89% / 58% hit (reproduces 09-01) vs extended names **−0.26% / 49%** (n=13,922; negative at T+5/T+10); geometry dead at T+5/T+10, +0.09% margin at T+20; CPCV's 73% pass is that one cell. Frog-in-the-Pan now holds (smooth +1.14% vs jumpy −0.57%), reversing the 09-01 index-only inversion. **§8.6: no promotion; origination scope is the owner's decision** (restrict to the eligible universe / pre-register a smooth-approach v2 / drop). **Decided 2026-09-09 (owner delegated, "best understanding"):** (1) the shadow's origination is RESTRICTED to the eligible universe from the 2026-09-10 sweep — the extended-name population measured no edge and would only dilute the validation sample; per the WO-18 rule a population change restarts the shadow clock (hi52 shadow soak restarts 2026-09-10). (2) **`hi52` v2 PRE-REGISTERED** (trial count N=2 with v1; `fold_pass_min(2)` = 60%): identical rule and parameters to v1 PLUS three signal-time filters whose thresholds are FIXED here from the 09-03 full-sample medians so they are knowable at signal time going forward — smooth approach: `up_day_frac ≥ 0.55` AND `max_day_move ≤ 0.07` over the 20 completed sessions ending at `y` (the `diagnostics_for` definitions); no gap day: trigger-session `|close(y)/close(y−1) − 1| ≤ 5%`; population: the eligible (index) universe. Backtest protocol: the same harness (`scripts/backtest_hi52.py --registration v2`), same costs/fills/horizons/CPCV, reported next to v1 never pooled; promotion bar unchanged (§6.4). The rank construct is not part of v2. Owner sign-off still gates any RECOMMEND wiring. **v1/v2 RUN 2026-09-09 16:31 (engine off, same window 2020-01-01→2026-09-09, 1,039 sessions, 3,205 symbols, veto applied; `data/reports/backtest_hi52_2026-09-09.json` and `backtest_hi52_v2_2026-09-09.json`, never pooled):** v1 discrete (16,121 trades, full market) — geometry dead at T+5/T+10, +0.10% margin at T+20 (median net +0.10% / 50.4%); index cell T+20 median net +1.54% / 56.4%; CPCV promotable at T+10/T+20 (fold 73%) on a population whose typical trade cannot pay the toll before T+20. v2 discrete (1,794 trades after 14,843 vetoes: smooth 11,405, gap 813, not_index 2,625) — geometry dead at T+5, **viable at T+10 (median net +0.62% / 54.0%) and T+20 (+1.71% / 58.2%)**; CPCV **promotable at T+10 (fold 80%) and T+20 (86.7%) under N=2**. Honest reading: the index restriction carries the edge (v2's T+20 ≈ v1's index cell), and inside v2 the smooth survivors (+1.48%) trail the jumpy survivors (+1.82%) at T+20 — the smooth/no-gap filters trim a third of the index population without raising the median; they are still the registered rule (no post-hoc relaxation, §6.4). §8.6 stands: promotion to an actionable ninth rule needs the shadow soak on the eligible population (restarted 2026-09-10) plus owner sign-off; the platform wires no expected edge on its own. **[SUPERSEDED 2026-09-12: the owner gate cleared and the promotion substituted the LIVE forward test for the remainder of that soak — see the `hi52` v2 PROMOTION and `hi52` KILL CRITERION addenda below. An expected edge IS wired, by owner direction, under a written demotion rule.]**

**`hi52` v2 PROMOTION — §8.6 owner gate cleared 2026-09-12 (owner-directed: "proceed with the changes you deem will improve the engine's performance"). PROMOTED AS A FORWARD TEST, NOT AS A VALIDATED EDGE.** The §8.6 bar for a ninth actionable rule was backtest + shadow soak + owner sign-off. The backtest is in (v2 run 2026-09-09, above); the soak on the restarted eligible population is NOT — it began 09-10 and has two sessions in it. This promotion deliberately substitutes the LIVE forward test for the remainder of that soak, at RECOMMEND-mode size, because the shadow cannot answer the question the backtest leaves open: the v2 edge is measured on an INDEX cell labelled by CURRENT membership applied backwards, which is a **survivorship-tainted proxy** — verbatim from the 09-03 and 09-09 runs, "a name that entered the index after a big run appears as an 'index member' throughout its climb, and a name that was dropped is mislabelled the other way" (`index_split_is_survivorship_tainted_proxy: true` in both JSONs) — and only a forward population is free of it. **What is wired (2026-09-12):** (i) the three v2 filters GATE in `hi52.scan_daily` where they were diagnostics — smooth approach (`up_day_frac >= 0.55` AND `max_day_move <= 0.07` over the 20 completed sessions ending `y`) and no gap day (`|close(y)/close(y-1) - 1| <= 0.05`), thresholds in `DEFAULT_PARAMS`, **owner-only and never learnable** (they define the measured population; a change is a new pre-registration, never a tuning), counted `VETO_SMOOTH`/`VETO_GAP` first-match on the `hi52_sweep` log line; the third filter (eligible population) was already the sweep's universe from 09-10. `hi52.V1_PARAMS` neutralizes the three so `scripts/backtest_hi52.py --registration v1` keeps reproducing the N=1 numbers. (ii) **Registered edge `hi52.expected_edge_pct = 1.47`** in `settings.yaml`, fed to the gate's `strategy_expected_edge_pct` exactly as `ins`'s is — owner-set, never learner-movable, and registered CONDITIONALLY: `Hi52Cfg.expected_edge_pct` defaults to **`None`**, so deleting the settings key genuinely un-registers the edge instead of falling back to a number no reader of `settings.yaml` can see — which is what makes step 2 of the kill criterion below a real edit rather than a no-op (2026-09-12 review finding). Derivation, stated because the number is not a transcription — and implemented ONCE, in code, as `scripts/hi52_forward_verdict.py` `registered_edge_pct()`, which a unit test pins against the shipped `settings.yaml` value so the registered edge and the kill criterion's cost floor cannot drift apart: `edge = measured T+20 median GROSS − CostModel.breakeven_pct(N, "CNC")`, `N = equity × per_trade_risk.swing_position_pct/100 ÷ (per_trade_risk.overnight_gap_mult × hi52 stop_pct/100)`. GROSS is **+2.0336%** (v2 discrete, n=1,794). `N` is the §7.1 cap on a swing position's notional — implied, never chosen: a swing position is capped at 2.0% of equity charged at 2.5× the stop distance against `hi52`'s 6% stop, so `N ≤ equity/7.5` (the price cancels out of `qty_max × price`) — **₹5,333** at the ₹40,000 base, **₹5,234** at the 2026-09-11 equity ₹39,256.65, **₹4,800** at the `equity_floor_rung` (₹36,000 = base −10%, the smallest equity at which the risk table still lets a position be OPENED: below it the platform goes flat and CLOSE_ONLY). `CostModel.breakeven_pct` there is 0.5303% / 0.5357% / 0.5619% — the floor RISES as the book shrinks — and this key is a constant read on every candidate for months, so it is derived at the WORST permitted book rather than today's: 2.0336 − 0.5619 = 1.4717 ⇒ **1.47**, rounded DOWN (rounding an edge up is the one direction that can buy a candidate a C3 pass it did not earn). Deliberately NOT the backtest's own +1.71% net: it charged 0.3192% at a ₹20,000 reference size this book never trades, so quoting it would credit `hi52` with a size it never gets. The first draft of this addendum registered **1.60** off a ₹8,000 notional the sizing rule cannot produce (0.4343%); the 2026-09-12 review refuted it and the manager fixed the basis at the §7.1-derived one. (iii) `hi52` **removed from `NO_EDGE_SHADOW_STRATEGIES`**, so C3 no longer rejects unconditionally. (iv) **Admission change:** the leg stops being the leftover-capacity SECOND `prescreen.admit` and joins the ONE ranked batch admission with `brk20`/`ins`/`cat`/`cat_reversal`, under the unchanged `max_per_strategy_day.hi52 = 3`. A rule that can reach RECOMMEND must contest the same slots as every other actionable leg; the 2026-08-18 fairness lesson is served by that single ranked admit, which is what it was built for. Three consequences on the record: `hi52` scores (~0.95-1.0) rank ABOVE `brk20`'s (~0.5-0.6) in that batch, so its cap is what bounds the displacement it can do; the leg's ~480-symbol × 400-session read now sits BEFORE the batch admit, which costs the admit that latency (the bar-driven `prescreen.sweep` still admits first and is untouched); and — the 2026-09-12 review's finding — that one ranked list decides **publication** as well as admission, which is a second and separable thing. `bus.apublish` order stamps `fired_at`, and `pipeline._forward_key` breaks ties INSIDE a per-strategy quantile band by `fired_at` ASCENDING — under `MIN_RANK_POPULATION` = 3 that band is every strategy's first two candidates of the day. Published on raw score, hi52's 0.95-1.0 would therefore take the earliest `fired_at` every morning and win the scarce analyst forward slot (4/day under the DG1-degraded cap) against `ins`, the platform's only validated leg — ties it LOST before the promotion, because its own second `admit` ran last. **Decided 2026-09-12: admission and publication are split.** The single ranked `prescreen.admit` still allocates the day CAP (that is what `_rank` is for), but `ops.main._publication_order` / `_PUBLICATION_LEG_ORDER` publishes the accepted batch in **LEG order — `brk20`, `ins`, `cat`, `cat_reversal`, `hi52`** — so cross-strategy RAW scores, which are not comparable (hi52 `prox` ∈ [0.95, 1.0]; `ins` ~0.5 at a bare ₹1cr crossing; `brk20` ~0.5-0.6), never set the forward queue's `fired_at` tie-break for any leg, not just for hi52. To be explicit about what that order does and does not decide: **the forward queue ranks by per-strategy QUANTILE band (`pipeline._quantile_band`, §5.2(a)); publication order only breaks ties INSIDE a band** — it cannot move a candidate between bands, and a strategy whose score scale is high buys nothing by it. A stable sort, so admit order inside each leg is untouched and §9.6 replay determinism holds (the key reads `strategy_id` and no clock); an id not in the tuple publishes last, deterministically, so adding a leg is a deliberate edit here rather than a silent reallocation of `fired_at`. The promotion buys hi52 real day-cap slots under its own cap; it does not buy queue priority over validated rules. (v) The analyst contract (`contracts._HI52`) is rewritten: forward-test framing, `target=None` BY DESIGN with the gate consuming the registered edge, decline grounds exhaustively mirroring `ins`, and **approach shape explicitly NOT a decline ground** — the rule already enforces smooth/no-gap, and inside the v2 population the smoother half did no better (+1.48% vs +1.82% at T+20), so judging approach shape a second time is second-guessing a gate that already ran.

**`hi52` KILL CRITERION (pre-registered 2026-09-12; the promotion is only honest with it).** Population: every `prescreen_day_slots` row with `strategy_id='hi52'` and `d >= 2026-09-14` (the first session under the promoted rule — an ASSUMPTION about when the tranche deploys, not a measurement: nothing in either store records the deploy, so the script prints the boundary's provenance with every verdict and `--from` overrides it. A window_open sweep run on or after 09-14 on pre-tranche code journals v1 rows that look identical, and pooling those would measure a rule the engine was not running) — every PUBLISHED signal, not the subset forwarded, recommended or bought; conditioning on the analyst's or the gate's later choices would measure them instead of the rule. Measurement (`scripts/hi52_forward_verdict.py`, read-only, always exit 0): entry = **the OPEN of the journal day `d` itself**, exit = the CLOSE of the **k-th session of the HOLD** (the entry session counting as the first), counted in SESSIONS of that symbol's own `bars_1d` so holidays and halts are handled by construction; net = gross minus one full CNC round trip from `CostModel` at the §7.1-SIZED notional, never a round number chosen by hand: `swing_position_pct` / (`overnight_gap_mult` × the 6% stop) = equity/7.5, with the two percentages read live from `limits.yaml` (so an owner change to either moves the floor without a code edit) and the equity taken at the **smallest** `equity_snapshots` reading in the measured window (₹5,234 ⇒ 0.5357% at the 09-11 equity; a shrinking book RAISES the floor rather than hiding behind a registration-day number, and integer-qty rounding leaves the charge a lower bound by a few hundredths of a pp). `--notional` can override the derivation for a what-if and the report then says **OVERRIDE**; charging ₹8,000 (0.4343%) as the first draft did under-costs every forward net by ~0.13 pp and biases BOTH demote clauses toward keeping an unvalidated rule trading — the 2026-09-12 review's finding. **The anchor is the BACKTEST's anchor** (manager decision 2026-09-12): the sweep that journals `d` runs on the session AFTER the trigger `y`, so `d` IS the study's entry session and `close(d + k−1)/open(d)` IS its `close(y+k)/open(y+1)` — the forward test measures the same quantity the registered edge was measured as, which is the only way it can refute it. Stated consequence: the **live fill lands LATER on `d`** (the recommendation goes out in the morning window and the owner executes by hand), so realized results trail this metric by that intraday drift; the metric is the RULE's drift, and a persistent gap between the two is an execution finding, never a reason to re-anchor the rule after the fact. **T+5 and T+10 are printed as DIAGNOSTICS, labelled as such** (T+5 at the manager's request — the v2 study measured it dead): the verdict reads T+20 alone. **Rule: DEMOTE if n >= 20 AND (median net at T+20 <= 0 OR hit rate at T+20 < 50%); HOLD otherwise; INSUFFICIENT below n = 20** (never HOLD — "not yet refuted" and "no evidence" must not read the same). Only the T+20 cell votes: it is the horizon the registered edge was measured over. **Review cadence: run the verdict at 20 and at 40 signals** (and at any owner request). **Demotion is two edits in ONE owner-visible commit citing the report: re-add `hi52.STRATEGY_ID` to `NO_EDGE_SHADOW_STRATEGIES` AND delete `hi52.expected_edge_pct` from `settings.yaml`** — either alone leaves a half-promoted rule (the shadow set wins a contradiction in the gate, but a dangling edge key reads as a live registration). BOTH are real edits: `Hi52Cfg.expected_edge_pct` defaults to `None` and the composition root registers the strategy only when the settings key is present, so deleting it genuinely un-registers the edge — the 2026-09-12 review's first finding was that a numeric model default would have made that step a no-op. **Operating note: the first 20 signals are VALIDATION, not income.** At the ~₹5,000 §7.1 sizes this rule at and 3 admissions a day the capital at risk is small by construction and is meant to be; a good or bad run of the first handful is noise against a T+20 median, and neither the edge, the thresholds, the cap nor the horizon may be moved in response to it — moving any of them restarts the clock and re-opens multiplicity. Nothing in §6.3 learning may touch `expected_edge_pct` or the three v2 thresholds, now or later.

**Slot-allocation addendum (owner-directed 2026-09-02, the third consecutive window-open cap-lockout session).** The 2026-08-27 displacement mechanism was bounded by its own stated LIMIT: `orb`'s score clamped at 1.0 (`min(1, vol_ratio/(2×vol_mult))` saturates from 2×-threshold volume up, which most post-range bars clear), so ranking and the displacement margin had nothing to discriminate with, and the window-open burst kept the whole sub-cap for the day — observed 08-27 (KALYANKJIL 1.0@12:49 refused), 09-01 (PERSISTENT/HCLTECH/INFY suppressed at cap), 09-02 (IDEA/VMM/OIL all score 1.0, 36/27/8 suppressions each, zero evaluations). Two paired changes: **(i) orb score de-saturation** — `vol_ratio/(vol_ratio + 2×vol_mult)`, a monotone squash (1/3 at threshold, 1.0 an asymptote never reached); a RANKING heuristic within the strategy, not a learnable parameter, claiming no edge. **(ii) `cap_release_schedule`** (`strategy.prescreen`, owner knob, never learner-movable): cumulative per-strategy sub-cap tranches by IST bar time (`orb: 3@10:00, 5@11:30, 7@13:00`) — effective cap = min(`max_per_strategy_day`, released tranche), so the schedule can only HOLD BACK capacity (every spam bound and displacement budget derived from the flat cap is untouched), midday/afternoon fires are guaranteed a contested seat, and displacement + de-saturated scores allocate WITHIN a tranche. Release time is the candidate's BAR time threaded from `_scan` (batch `admit` keeps flat caps — no bar exists there); no Clock is read, so §9.6 replay determinism holds by construction. Rollback: remove the schedule key (flat caps) and/or the score formula is a one-line revert. Deliberately NOT done: raising the orb cap (24 consecutive analyst declines of the class across 09-01/09-02 — more evaluations of a gross-negative-tested class buy nothing) and any decrement/shrink path for released tranches (the spam bound forbids it).

**Declined-incumbent displacement + measured digest for the planner + SDK result text (owner-directed 2026-09-04, from the 09-03 movers forensics — the first session on the slot-allocation addendum above).** Evidence: 4,090 `orb` fires → 10 slots → 6 forwards → 5 `no_action` → 0 entries; from 09:52 (3 slots) and 11:30:07 (5 slots) `displaceable=0` on 3,984 refusals — UNITDSPR 0.967, GMRAIRPORT 0.895, KEI 0.825, BSE 0.811 never reached the analyst — because `claim_slot` makes a pair permanent and a `no_action` verdict never un-made it; 6 of the day's 48 forwards were used. **(a)** `SignalPreScreen.decline(symbol, strategy)`, called by the pipeline on a `no_action` verdict: the pair stays SEEN (its publication is spent — this is not the 2026-07-29 infrastructure re-arm, and the journal row stays `evaluated=1`) but is a displacement victim again under the same margin and order. Evicting a declined incumbent is a NEW evaluation, so it is charged to the day-level displacement budget and bounded by the forward cap, not to the per-strategy churn budget (5/5 spent by 11:30 on 09-03, which would otherwise have re-locked the afternoon). Hydrated pairs stay permanent (the existing restart carve-out). **(b)** The digest rail: `sentiment_agg.market` read −1.000 on 09-02 and 09-03 from raw −9.48 across 759 clusters (≈ −0.01 per cluster — neutral flow); the analyst context carried the WO-22 measure but `preopen_planner._digest_lines` rendered the bare value, the plan called it "pinned at its floor", and every verdict cited a hostile tape on a day NIFTY closed −0.12% with 104/97 breadth. One shared `sentiment_rail_note` now annotates both contexts and adds the per-cluster mean; `value` itself is unchanged (the `cat` shadow's frozen thresholds read it). **(c)** Harness: a CLI result carrying `is_error` fails with the CLI's own `result` text — the SDK reported "error result: success" for the 31 usage-limit failures of 09-03 (12:42–14:32 and 19:07–19:28, the subscription's session window shared with dev sessions) — and limit text classifies as `overloaded`. Process rule in `CLAUDE.md`: no agent fan-outs 09:15–15:30 IST on trading days. NOT built, deliberately: a trend-continuation intraday leg. Hindsight replay from raw ticks of the day's 11 ORB candidates at ORB geometry: −4.78% net (the five declines −3.5%, IDFCFIRSTB the only winner); the owner's six bought at first fire and held to squareoff: 5/6 positive, +0.7…+2.0% net. That is a different strategy with no evidence yet — it needs a pre-registered backtest under the §6.1 protocol before any origination change.
