**O15 — eligible universe widened to NIFTY 500 (owner-directed 2026-09-04: "extend the eligible universe to nifty 500").** Decision recorded with the evidence the manager raised against it the same day, so the trade-off is on the record: the extended-name hi52 thesis was refuted (T+20 −0.26%), three intraday price-only families are refuted at retail cost, the `ins` edge was validated on the NIFTY200-eligible population (the NIFTY500 tail is out-of-sample for it), and the small-cap movers the owner keeps citing are the population where after-the-move continuation measured worst. The owner chose scope over that evidence; the platform's job is to apply the same rules and the same gates to the wider set and keep measuring. **What changes:** the index list that seeds the §3.2.4 rule becomes NIFTY 500 (`universe.index_name` / `index_source_url` / `index_seed_path`, seed `config/universe/nifty500_seed.csv`); eligible = NIFTY 500 ∩ MIS ∩ surveillance-clean ∩ ₹5cr median traded value (expect ~350–450 names); the extended leg's candidates become criteria-passing names OUTSIDE NIFTY 500 (marker `not_in_index`, superseding `not_nifty200`; the store accepts both markers on read for the 09-01…09-04 rows). **What deliberately does not change:** `data.universe_max_watchlist` stays 200, so the per-bar intraday watchlist is the top 200 eligible by traded value (≈ the large caps) — raising it is a separate, measured step because tick volume, bar building and per-minute feature snapshots scale with it (the 08-24 loop-starvation and today's store-stall class); every swing/batch leg (`brk20`, `ins`, `hi52`, `cat`) reads the full eligible set at once. `sector_map` runs over the batch universe so widened names are classified rather than falling into the UNCLASSIFIED cap-1 bucket (§7.1). **Gate membership = the ELIGIBLE set, not the focus watchlist (audit finding during implementation):** `RiskGate._universe_row` read `universe_daily` with `included_only=True`, which was equivalent to "eligible" only while the cap did not bind; under O15 it would have rejected every candidate from the ~200 eligible-but-capped names as out-of-universe and made the widening a no-op for swing recommendations. The gate now accepts `included` rows and rows excluded for `watchlist_cap` alone, and still rejects extended-leg rows (`not_in_index` / legacy `not_nifty200`) — the RECOMMEND boundary is the eligible set by definition; the cap governs ticks only. Caps, the catalyst guard, limits and the analyst are untouched. **Shadow populations:** `cat`/`cat_reversal` (verdict ~mid-Oct) and `hi52` measure on the eligible set, which changes on the first NIFTY 500 build; their verdicts are computed on both populations, before/after that date. **Of the owner's cited names:** IDEA and NIACL are in NIFTY 500; PCJEWELLER, JINDWORLD and PAISALO are not and remain extended-only.

**`tdc` pre-registration — trend-day continuation, intraday (owner-directed 2026-09-04: "will the flagged tickers be recommended under the updated logic? If not, find the solution").** Answer first, evidence-backed: NO. A tranche-faithful replay of 09-03's in-window `orb` stream through the new allocation (`decline()` + displacement, `scripts`-free scratch replay, pipeline modelled as one forward per 3 min and every verdict `no_action`) yields six evaluations instead of five (INDUSINDBK, COROMANDEL, TATACONSUM, INDUSTOWER, IDFCFIRSTB, TATACOMM); of the owner's six, only IDFCFIRSTB reaches the analyst — exactly as on the day. BSE/KEI/GMRAIRPORT/UNITDSPR/CGPOWER are never admitted because `orb`'s score is a single-bar volume-ratio squash that ranks spike names (BHARATFORG 1.0, closed −2.5%; TATACOMM 0.995) above steady climbers, and a 0.10 margin against ~0.9 incumbents is unreachable. Even when admitted, `orb`'s geometry (stop at the OR low, target 1.5R from the fire price) turns a late fire into a wide stop and a far target, which the analyst declines correctly (hindsight replay 09-03: the 11 candidates at ORB geometry −4.78% net; the six bought at first fire and held to 15:15 +0.7…+2.0% net on 5/6). The names the owner keeps flagging are trend-day continuation setups, and no validated leg captures that shape. **Hypothesis H1 (pre-registered, no sweep):** on a session where a NIFTY200 stock shows ACCEPTANCE above its opening range with sustained PARTICIPATION and RELATIVE STRENGTH by late morning, the move continues into the close net of MIS costs. **Rule, all at the 11:00 IST bar close T:** (i) close > session VWAP and > OR high (09:15–09:30), and the last 15 one-minute closes all > VWAP; (ii) `rel_volume_tod` ≥ 1.5 (cumulative volume to T ÷ the median cumulative volume to the same minute over the prior 20 sessions); (iii) return from open ≥ +1.0% and ≥ NIFTY 50's return from open + 1.0%; entry = next 1m open after T; exit = first 1m close < VWAP(T) × (1 − 0.25%) (exit at the following open), else 15:15 squareoff; no target; rank by relative strength, top 5 per day. **Variant B (the owner's observed pattern):** same selection, hold to 15:15 with no stop. **Robustness variants, reported never selected:** T ∈ {10:30, 12:00}; RVOL ∈ {1.2, 2.0}; stop ∈ {0.5%}. **Data:** `bars_1m` from the 1m floor 2025-07-10 to yesterday, NIFTY200 eligible set (as `orb`), NIFTY 50 1m for RS, `bars_1d` for the 20-session volume medians; costs = `CostModel.breakeven_pct` for MIS plus one tick of slippage each side. **Splits:** by index regime (NIFTY up/down from open at T) and by breadth (share of the universe above its OR high at T ≥ 50% = trend day). **Metrics & promotion (§8.6):** n, mean net %, win rate, t-stat, CPCV (`engine.learning.validate.cpcv_splits`, as `hi52`); promotable iff mean net > 0 with t > 2 on n ≥ 200 AND positive in ≥ 60% of CPCV splits; anything less is a refutation and no origination changes — the honest answer to the owner then becomes "these rallies are not capturable by a rule we can validate at retail costs". **Prior that argues against H1, recorded:** the `orb` v2 backtest found a negative-gross intraday fade in 2025-H2. Report: `data/reports/backtest_tdc_<date>.json`; harness `scripts/backtest_tdc.py`. Run requires the DuckDB writer stopped (evening, engine idle — owner-permitted DB-task stop). **RESULT (run 2026-09-04 14:0x on the owner-stopped engine, `data/reports/backtest_tdc_2026-09-04.json`, 30 harness tests green, manager-audited for look-ahead at the entry/exit/VWAP/RVOL sites): REFUTED in every variant × split cell — §8.6 verdict: not promotable, no origination change.** Window 2023-07-17 → 2026-09-03 (775 sessions; `bars_1m` runs deeper than the 2025-07-10 floor the paragraph assumed, and the plan-window cell is reported separately with the same sign). H1: n=3,370, mean net −0.129%, median −0.41%, win 39.6%, t=−4.63, CPCV positive 0/15; B (hold to 15:15): n=3,370, −0.139%, t=−4.33; every robustness cell negative (T=10:30 −0.082%, T=12:00 −0.108%, RVOL 1.2/2.0 −0.129/−0.120%, stop 0.5% −0.123%, sector-confirmed C −0.088%). The mechanism: H1's mean GROSS is +0.035% against a mean cost of 0.164% — the same cost-geometry shape as the two `orb` refutations; late-morning continuation has essentially no expected move before costs. Two thin cells kept on record, neither a finding: (a) breadth ≥ 50% of the universe above its OR high at T (true trend days) is positive in every variant (B: n=108, +0.46% net, t=2.5, CPCV 100%) but n≈110 is under the 200 floor, and such days are rare (breadth averages ~0.21); (b) `catalyst_at_T=true` beats `false` in all seven variants (H1: +0.04% vs −0.20%, n=46/69, 2026-07-10 onward only). Either may be pre-registered as its own hypothesis later; neither may be selected from this run. Protocol deviations, all stated in the report: full history instead of the 2025-07-10 floor; equal-weight universe return as the index proxy before 2026-07-23 (real NIFTY 50 1m exists on 30 sessions only; reported separately); acceptance pinned to the running VWAP per minute; CPCV purge/embargo at the §6.4 defaults (single-session trades do not overlap); `sector_map` applied backwards from its 2026-07/08 snapshots; sessions enumerated from `bars_1m` because `config/calendar` has no 2023 file. Side finding: `NSECalendar` calls the weekend Union-Budget sessions (2025-02-01, 2026-02-01) non-trading — a calendar gap to fix independently.

**Candle / price-action battery pre-registration (owner-directed 2026-09-04, after the `tdc` refutation: "can't we track the candles and trade based on their movements?").** Five long-only 5-minute rules × two exit styles, every cell reported, none selected, no sweeps — the protocol is in `runbooks/briefs/candles_backtest_brief_2026-09-04.md` and is binding: R1 momentum burst (close above the prior six 5m highs on ≥ 2× median volume, closing in the top quarter of its range), R2 three soldiers (three bullish bars each closing above the prior high), R3 engulfing after a ≥ 3-bar pullback that held VWAP, R4 VWAP-hold retest buy, R5 VWAP reclaim on volume; exits E1 (stop at the signal bar low, target 2R, else 15:15) and E2 (trail: exit on the first 5m close below the prior bar's low). Signals 09:45–14:00, entry at the next 1m open, one trade per symbol-day per rule, top 5 per rule per day by volume ratio, MIS cost + 1 tick/side, splits by index regime, 11:00 breadth and catalyst_at_T; promotion rule as `tdc`. Harness `scripts/backtest_candles.py`, report `data/reports/backtest_candles_<date>.json`. The prior: two `orb` refutations and `tdc`'s +0.035% gross say intraday price-only signals in this universe carry no edge at retail cost; the battery exists so that claim is tested against the rules a discretionary trader would actually use, not assumed. **RESULT (run 2026-09-04, 755 sessions 2023-07-17 → 2026-09-03, `data/reports/backtest_candles_2026-09-04.json`, 41 harness tests green, two trades hand-verified against raw bars, manager-audited): REFUTED in all 10 rule × exit cells and every split — §8.6 verdict: not promotable.** n ≈ 3,600–3,760 per cell; mean net −0.14% to −0.18%; win rates 21–36%; t between −8.9 and −20.6; CPCV positive 0/6 in every pooled cell. The decisive row is the gross: every cell's mean GROSS return lies between −0.03% and +0.01% against a 0.14–0.15% cost — unlike `tdc` (+0.035% gross) there is no edge for cost to erode; the candle rules are noise at 5-minute resolution in this universe. Neither the trend-day breadth cell nor the catalyst cell rescues any rule (the catalyst cells hold 12–20 trades and are mostly negative). Optimistic assumptions disclosed by the harness cut the other way: stop fills at the stop price on gap-through minutes (R5|E1 14.5% of trades), survivorship in the symbol set, and a volume-ratio daily cap that picks the day's most active 5.7% of triggers. The two weekend Budget sessions are kept; the 23 watchlist-ramp sessions (2026-07-20 → 08-19) are dropped by the > 150-symbol floor, which is why the real-index and catalyst splits are thin.

**R3 — ATR-conditioned population split on the two intraday harnesses, PRE-REGISTERED 2026-09-12 (written BEFORE the run; evidence only, wires nothing).** The 2026-09-11 rebuttal to the intraday refutations was that intraday alpha lives in high-beta / high-ATR names, and that every refuted family — `orb` (0/15 twice), `tdc` (2026-09-04), the candle battery (the paragraph above) — was measured on the POOLED NIFTY 200/500 universe where a quiet majority drowns the movers. That is a testable claim about the POPULATION, not a new rule, and it is tested here as a SPLIT of the results already registered: no threshold moves, no variant/rule/exit is added, no trade is added or removed, no cell is selected. **The statistic:** Wilder ATR(14) of DAILY bars (`bars_1d`, via the repo's own `engine.strategy.indicators.wilder_atr`) over the 14 completed sessions STRICTLY BEFORE the signal day, divided by the close of the last of those sessions, in percent — the window ends the session before the signal, so a signal day's own range never enters its own label. Pinned reading: the window is exactly 14 sessions, so the earliest bar in it has no prior close inside it and its true range is the plain high−low of the repo's `_true_range`; the gap-aware alternative was not computed, so no selection between the two took place. **The cut:** terciles of the full measured population, taken ONCE over the distinct measured (symbol, signal day) pairs so a symbol-day sits in the same cell under every variant and the cells compare populations rather than cuts (`scripts/backtest_brk20.py`'s margin-tercile posture, verbatim in force). A symbol-day with fewer than 14 prior daily sessions is labelled `atr_pct_unclassified` and KEPT, so the four ATR cells sum to the pooled cell exactly. **Reported for** `tdc` H1 and B and for every candle rule × exit cell — n, mean/median gross and net, hit rate, t-stat and CPCV positive share in each cell (the harnesses compute every split for every variant, so the `tdc` robustness variants' rows print too; the registered read is H1 and B). **Promotion rule unchanged and evaluated IN THE CELL:** mean net % > 0 AND t > 2 AND n ≥ 200 AND CPCV positive-split share ≥ 0.60. **DESCRIPTIVE, NOT TRADEABLE:** the cuts are full-sample statistics of the measured population and were unknowable at signal time — exactly the caveat `hi52`'s smooth/jumpy cut and `brk20`'s margin tercile carry. **The prior argues against the claim:** cost geometry, not signal quality, killed the pooled cells — the candle battery's mean GROSS sits between −0.03% and +0.01% against a 0.14–0.15% cost, and `tdc` H1's gross was +0.035% against 0.164% — so a high-ATR cell can rescue a family only if volatility buys GROSS drift faster than it widens the spread the cost model already charges; a wider range at the same drift is not an edge, and a high-ATR cell whose gross is still ≈ 0 refutes the rebuttal rather than supporting it. **Survivorship caveat, the one direction that could manufacture a false positive:** the terciles are cut over the names `bars_1m` holds today under today's index membership applied backwards, so the high-ATR cell holds the SURVIVING high-volatility names — a name whose volatility took it out of the universe is not in the population at all. **Multiplicity:** this is a split of two already-registered studies, not a new trial; nothing is promoted on the strength of a cell, and a promotable cell would be an input to a fresh pre-registration, never a deployment. Harnesses `scripts/backtest_tdc.py` and `scripts/backtest_candles.py` (reporting-only addition; no other change to either), reports `data/reports/backtest_tdc_2026-09-12.json` (+`.md`) and `data/reports/backtest_candles_2026-09-12.json` (+`.md`). **RESULT (run 2026-09-12, engine stopped, DuckDB read-only; `tdc` 781 sessions 2023-07-17 → 2026-09-11 and the candle battery 761 sessions over the same span — both windows run to 09-11 rather than the 09-04 run's 09-03, so every pooled cell is re-stated in the reports beside its ATR cells; 37 `tests/unit/test_backtest_tdc.py` + 50 `tests/unit/test_backtest_candles.py` green): the rebuttal is REFUTED ON NET in both harnesses — `promotable=True` in NO cell of either study, ATR cells included. §8.6 verdict: not promotable, nothing wired.** `tdc`: 6,639 measured symbol-days, cuts 2.5578% / 3.4561%, ATR% spread 1.04–12.92%, 4 symbol-days unclassified. H1 top tercile n=1,164, mean gross **+0.0821%** against a pooled **+0.0347%**, mean net −0.1003%, hit 39.5%, t −1.65, CPCV+ 13%; B top tercile n=1,164, gross **+0.1030%** against a pooled +0.0245%, net −0.0794%, hit 44.7%, t −1.11, CPCV+ 20%. So the population claim is PARTLY REAL ON GROSS — this is the first intraday cell the project has measured in which the high-volatility subset carries visibly more drift than the pooled population, and it is recorded as such — and it changes no verdict: **volatility buys gross drift and cost at nearly the same rate.** The top tercile pays 0.1824% per round trip against 0.1507% in the bottom one (high-ATR names here are the cheap ones and the one-tick-per-side slippage term is price-relative), so of the +0.0589 pp gross advantage over the bottom tercile only +0.0272 pp reaches net, against a 0.1263% floor. The mid tercile is the WORST cell on both variants (−0.1594% / −0.1999% net), so ATR and net are not monotone — there is no clean "more volatility, more edge" gradient to build on. Candle battery: 15,038 measured symbol-days, cuts 2.3291% / 3.0857%; across all ten rule × exit cells the top tercile's mean GROSS runs −0.0796% to +0.0330% against a 0.150–0.159% cost in that same cell, mean net −0.2386% to −0.1263%, every t between −9.32 and −4.04, CPCV+ never above 7%. `R2` (three soldiers) is WORSE in the high tercile than the low one on both exits (−0.0796% vs +0.0202% gross on E1), so for that rule the split runs against the rebuttal outright; the 09-04 verdict "there is no edge for cost to erode" survives the split intact. `orb`, `tdc` and the candle battery therefore stay refuted at retail cost in the high-ATR population as well as the pooled one; the one finding worth carrying forward is the cost-scaling arithmetic above, which says any future high-volatility intraday thesis must clear a LARGER toll, not the pooled one.

**R4 — the SHORT / fade side of intraday, PRE-REGISTERED 2026-09-12 (written BEFORE the run; evidence only, wires nothing).** Every intraday sweep this platform has ever run was LONG-ONLY under the §1.4.9 short gate — `orb` (0/15 twice), `tdc` (2026-09-04), the candle battery (2026-09-04), the WO-10b VWAP-deviation study (aborted at stage 1) — so the fade side is not a refuted hypothesis here, it is an UNTESTED one, and the 2026-09-11 verification recorded it as one of the two untested intraday candidates (the other is R3, above). The prior FOR it is our own negative: the `orb` v2 backtest found a **negative-gross fade of breakouts in 2025-H2** — the thing that killed the long side is exactly what this study measures head-on. **TWO rules, fixed here, TRIAL COUNT N = 2; no sweep, no variant search, no "best of"** (`fold_pass_min(2)` = 60%, which coincides with `tdc`'s flat 60% CPCV bar, so the promotion rule is unambiguous under either reading). **F1 — failed-breakout fade,** every clause read at the **11:00** IST decision bar T: a name whose 1m CLOSE broke ABOVE its opening-range high (the 09:15–09:30 range, i.e. the COMPLETED bars 09:15..09:29) at some bar in **[09:30, 10:30)**, with `rel_volume_tod` ≥ **1.5**, and whose **11:00 close is back BELOW the OR high**. **F2 — gap fade,** at the **10:00** bar T: the **09:15 open ≥ +2.0% above the prior close** AND the **10:00 close below the session open**. **Both:** SHORT at the OPEN of the next 1m bar (11:01 / 10:01); stop = the **session high so far** (max high 09:15..T, fixed at entry, never trailed); ONE exit — the first 1m close ABOVE the fade-failure level (F1: the OR high; F2: the 10:00 bar's high), filled at the FOLLOWING bar's open — else the **15:15** squareoff, whichever comes first; no target; **no per-day cap and no ranking**, because the rule names no selection criterion and inventing one would be a free parameter (so a wide-participation session contributes many correlated trades, which is why the CPCV observation unit is the per-SESSION mean and the per-trade t-stat is reported with that caveat attached). **Pinned readings — two ambiguities resolved before the run, with the alternative NOT computed in either case, so no selection between readings took place:** (i) `rel_volume_tod` is measured AT T — the `tdc` definition verbatim (cumulative volume 09:15..T over the median cumulative volume to the SAME minute across the 20 prior sessions; fewer than 10 usable priors ⇒ the symbol-day is SKIPPED and counted, never admitted on a thin denominator) — not at the breakout minute; (ii) the stop is evaluated on the 1m CLOSE and filled at the next open, the house trigger/fill convention. **The stop is DOMINATED by arithmetic, and the report states that rather than hiding it:** the session high through T is by construction ≥ the OR high and ≥ the T bar's high, so any close above the stop is also a close above the failure level and the failure exit fires first at the same price; `n_exits_stop_first` is reported and must be 0 for both registered rules. **Population:** the ELIGIBLE universe (the latest `universe_daily` day's `included` rows plus `exclusion_reasons == ['watchlist_cap']` rows — NIFTY 500-based since O15) applied BACKWARDS and intersected with the symbols `bars_1m` holds that session, i.e. the ~200-name tick watchlist: a **survivorship-tainted proxy** in exactly the sense `brk20` and `hi52` carry, and for a SHORT study the sign of that bias is not obvious (a name in today's NIFTY 500 is a name that did not collapse), so it is reported, not corrected, and the JSON carries `population_is_survivorship_tainted_proxy: true`. **Window:** the full `bars_1m` history (2023-07-17 onward). **Costs:** one MIS round trip from `CostModel.breakeven_pct(₹20,000, "MIS")` — statutory fees plus the WO-2 measured spread — plus **one tick of slippage on EACH side**, charged price-relative. A short is the same two legs in the reverse order and the per-side components (intraday STT on the sell, stamp duty on the buy) are each charged once either way, so the round trip is side-agnostic; MIS never CNC, a short leg cannot be a delivery position. **Not modelled and material — SHORTABILITY:** a T2T/GSM/ASM surveillance bucket, a broker MIS block or an F&O ban makes a name unshortable on the day, and the eligible snapshot screens those flags as of its LATEST day only, so every cell here is an UPPER bound on what the short side could actually have been traded at. **Universal ex-date veto:** any `corp_actions` ex-date in the 35 calendar days ending at `d` vetoes the symbol-day for BOTH rules (Kite minute candles are adjusted at fetch time — §14 Q10 — so a stitched series can hold two unit systems, and a split multiplies the 20-session volume denominator either way); F2 additionally vetoes a prior close older than 7 calendar days (a suspension, not a gap) and a >3% disagreement between the `bars_1d` prior close and the prior session's last 1m close (a unit mismatch, well clear of the ~0.5% by which the NSE's 30-minute closing VWAP routinely differs from the 15:29 print). **Splits:** index regime (NIFTY 50 return from open at T, with the equal-weight eligible-universe mean as the proxy before the real 1m index series starts and the real-index subset reported as its own cell), **R3's ATR tercile** (the same statistic and the same four cells, cut once per rule family so a symbol-day sits in the same cell under every variant of its rule), and calendar year. **Reporting-only cells — never selected, never counted in N = 2:** F1 with the decision bar at **12:00**; F2 with a **15:15-only** exit (no failure exit and no stop); and, answering the execution question the close trigger structurally cannot, F1 and F2 with the **stop evaluated on the 1m HIGH**, filled at `max(stop, that bar's open)` and allowed to fire on the squareoff bar itself — a change that can only make a fade look WORSE. **Promotion rule:** `tdc`'s, verbatim — mean net % > 0 AND t > 2 AND n ≥ 200 AND CPCV positive-split share ≥ 0.60 (`engine.learning.validate.cpcv_splits`, purge/embargo at the §6.4 defaults, which are already strictly more conservative than single-session trades need). **A fail is a REFUTATION and changes nothing.** **Whatever it finds, shorts stay gated for AUTO by §1.4.9:** a promotable cell would be an input to a separate, freshly pre-registered origination proposal plus an owner decision (§8.6), never a wiring. Harness `scripts/backtest_fade.py` — which IMPORTS `scripts/backtest_tdc.py`'s read-only DB attach, HH:MM helpers, `rel_volume_tod` medians, cost function and CPCV/metrics/promotion boolean rather than copying them, so the two intraday studies cannot drift apart in definition — report `data/reports/backtest_fade_2026-09-12.json` (+`.md`). Run requires the DuckDB writer stopped (engine idle — owner-permitted DB-task stop). **RESULT (run 2026-09-12 18:35 on the stopped engine, DuckDB read-only, `data/reports/backtest_fade_2026-09-12.json` + `.md`; window 2023-07-17 → 2026-09-11, 781 sessions, 315 of 480 eligible symbols carrying 1m bars; 44 `tests/unit/test_backtest_fade.py` green and 31 trades of an August-2026 slice independently re-derived from raw `bars_1m`/`bars_1d` with ZERO mismatches): REFUTED — `promotable=True` in NO cell of either registered rule, and in no reporting-only cell either. §8.6 verdict: not promotable, no origination change; shorts stay gated for AUTO by §1.4.9 regardless.** F1 (failed-breakout fade): n=**5,491** over 752 signal days, mean GROSS **+0.0500%** against a mean cost of **0.1526%** ⇒ mean net **−0.1026%**, median **−0.3002%**, hit 34.8%, t **−7.54**, CPCV positive **0/15**. F2 (gap fade): n=**1,819** over 441 signal days, gross **+0.0258%** against **0.1777%** ⇒ net **−0.1519%**, median −0.3006%, hit 10.5%, t −7.24, CPCV 13%. **The mechanism is the same cost geometry that killed the long side, now measured from the other direction, and it is a different finding from the candle battery's:** fading a failed breakout DOES carry a small positive expected move before costs — +0.05%, about a third of one round trip — so the short side is not noise the way the 5-minute candle rules were (gross ≈ 0); it is a real edge one third the size of its toll. That is the symmetric answer to the 2025-H2 `orb` fade prior: the fade is real and it is too small, exactly as the breakout was. **F2's registered geometry is a hair-trigger, which is the honest reading of its 10.5% hit rate rather than a defence of it:** the failure level is the 10:00 bar's own HIGH, whose median distance from the 10:01 entry is **+0.0688%** — half the 0.1263% fee floor, and inside one tick on a cheap name — and 298 of the 1,819 trades entered AT or ABOVE it, so 1,623 (89%) exit on the failure. Registered F2 is in effect a short with a 7-basis-point stop; the exit was fixed before that geometry was measured and is NOT re-cut after the fact. What the gap-fade THESIS looks like without the hair-trigger is the registered reporting cell `F2_1515_only` (same entries, held to 15:15): mean net **−0.3430%**, median −0.1990%, hit 44.8% — still refuted, now with a fat left tail (the gap-ups that kept running) in place of many small losses. **Splits.** ATR, on R3's statistic and cut construction: F1's WILDEST tercile is its WORST cell (gross **+0.0132%** vs **+0.0745%** in the calmest, cost 0.1627% vs 0.1456%, net −0.1495% vs −0.0711%) — the high-volatility rebuttal runs against the SHORT side too, and for the reason R3 measured, that volatility buys cost at least as fast as drift. Index regime: F1 fades better on a falling tape (gross +0.0631% vs +0.0369%) and never enough; F2 is gross-NEGATIVE on a falling tape (−0.027%) and gross-positive on a rising one (+0.121%) — the opposite shape, and not a stable one. Year: both decay monotonically — F1's gross runs +0.0864% (2023) → +0.0180% (2026), F2's +0.1546% → **−0.0369%**, i.e. the gap fade is gross-negative in 2025 and 2026. The real-index cells (79 / 25 trades) are thin and carry no weight. **Execution conservatism — the check that could have discredited the whole study:** evaluating the stop on the 1m HIGH instead of the close moves 155 of 5,491 F1 trades and 5 of 1,819 F2 trades and makes both rules very slightly BETTER (net −0.1003% / −0.1508%), so the registered close-trigger/next-open convention is not flattering the result. The dominated-stop invariant held on live data: `n_exits_stop_first` = 0 and `stop < exit level` = 0 in every close-trigger cell. **The ONLY positive cell with n ≥ 200 anywhere in the study, stated with both reasons it is not a finding:** `F2_1515_only` × `index_up_at_entry` — n=649, mean net **+0.151%**, CPCV positive **80%** — but t = **1.94**, which FAILS the registered t > 2 bar, and it is a split of a REPORTING-ONLY cell, so it is doubly ineligible and no post-hoc reading may promote it; anyone who wants it needs a fresh pre-registration on its own population. **Caveats that cut against the numbers rather than for them:** the population is the survivorship-tainted eligible proxy intersected with the ~200-name tick watchlist; SHORTABILITY is assumed, not proven (a surveillance bucket or an F&O ban would remove exactly the names most worth fading), so every cell is an UPPER bound; the index regime is the equal-weight universe proxy on 745 of 781 sessions; the universal ex-date veto removed 24,618 symbol-days and F2's cross-source guards a further 36 (unit mismatch) + 9 (no prior close); and with no per-day cap a wide-participation session contributes many correlated trades, so the per-trade t-stats are optimistic about independence and the per-session CPCV shares (0% and 13%) are the numbers that are not. **Intraday now has four refuted families rather than three** — `orb`, `tdc`, the candle battery and the fade side — and the §8.6 bar for re-enabling any intraday origination is unchanged.

**WO-20 (2026-08-20, owner-directed origination review): the analyst judges the strategy that fired, not the day-trade it imagines — truthful inputs + strategy-scoped mandate.** The review's funnel autopsy (08-13→08-19) found the entire recommendation drought concentrated at ONE stage: 63/63 analyst evaluations ended `no_action`; zero proposals have ever been created; the §7.1 gate has never been invoked (0 `gate_verdict` lines all-time). The declines decompose into (i) two inputs that lie and (ii) a rubric that structurally cannot pass the strategy shapes the platform validated. Evidence, pinned: **(E1)** `features.rel_volume` = cumulative session volume ÷ 20d median FULL-DAY volume with no time-of-day adjustment (`features/engine.py`), so every early-session candidate reads 0.02–0.24; the analyst demonstrably misreads it as time-adjusted ("roughly 3% of normal participation for this time of day" — ICICIBANK 08-14, rowid 750) and cited phantom thinness in 44/63 declines. **(E2)** `sentiment_agg` is a clipped decay-weighted SUM that saturated at a rail on 7/17 digest days (±1.000 on 08-04/05/06/14/17/18/19) — a few same-direction headlines reach the rail, yet the analyst reads "−1.000, the floor of the scale" as extreme regime evidence (46/63 declines cite regime). **(E3)** Null-target strategies are structurally unproposable: rsi2 (indicator exit) and ins (time exit) carry `target=None` BY DESIGN, and the analyst declines on "no target ⇒ reward cannot be framed ⇒ I won't invent one" — 52/63 declines were exactly these two strategies; the §7.1 gate already has the `strategy_expected_edge_pct` seam for this exact case, but candidates die one layer before it. **(E4)** Swing candidates are judged on intraday microstructure (five 1m bars, VWAP position, OR completeness at 09:20) — a category error for 5–20-session holds; the 08-19 HCLTECH `ins` decline (the only validated-edge candidate ever originated) cited below-VWAP/downtrend/day-plan-avoid — the *expected population* for insider-crossing events, i.e. the deployment re-imposed the unvalidated discretionary filter the WO-16 validation deliberately had none of. Note the counterweight, recorded honestly: the analyst's cost/geometry reasoning repeatedly matched our own backtest findings (MFSL noise-stop, DRREDDY 10-ATR stop) — orb/brk20/trend/mom declines are largely CORRECT calls on unproven legs; the defect is confined to lying inputs and a single intraday frame applied to all classes. **The fix, four parts: (a)** a per-strategy CONTRACT block in every signal-candidate context (`engine/strategy/contracts.py`, rendered by `ContextAssembler.for_signal`): timeframe class, exit mechanism, reward basis (validated expected edge where one exists), evidence status (validated vs exploratory — stated honestly), entry-anchor semantics (ins/brk20 stale-reference is design, not weakness), and the strategy's OWN disqualifier list (for counter-trend/event legs: fresh materially-negative catalyst, surveillance flags, results-day ban, liquidity collapse — NOT red-tape/below-VWAP/plan-avoid-as-momentum-advice); **(b)** `intraday.SYSTEM_PROMPT` rule 12 rewritten to bind judgement to the candidate's contract frame + a new rule "a null target with a stated time/indicator exit is not missing reward; never decline solely on a null target" + deletion of the false "earned their edge over years" claim (evidence status now lives per-contract; prompt stays a byte-stable constant); **(c)** input honesty at render time: `_sentiment_line` labels rail values as SATURATED (clipped-sum semantics legend), `_features_text` appends a rel_volume legend naming the full-day denominator; **(d)** forward-drain re-queue on transport-cancelled analyst calls (08-18 KALYANKJIL: SDK `CancelledError` mid-call left a forwarded candidate with no `agent_calls` row — invisible to every DB measure; bounded single re-queue + loud journal, and engine-shutdown cancellation still propagates). The §7.1 gate is deliberately untouched — it remains the hard disposal layer, and nothing here weakens a limit. **Filed follow-ups, not in this WO:** time-normalized `rel_volume_tod` (cum-volume vs 20d median cum-volume at same elapsed minutes, from bars_1m) to replace the legend with a correct number; digest storage of the UNCLIPPED sum + cluster count so saturation is measurable, not just labelled.

**WO-21 (2026-08-20 evening, day-one validation of WO-20): the guidance-schema extras trap generalized shut + two ops hardenings.** WO-20's first live day produced the platform's FIRST `enter` outputs ever (brk20 ICICIAMC + POLICYBZR, 12:10–12:15) — and both died on `extra_forbidden`: the FLAT structured-output schema (§8.1 — the CLI degrades to text on unions, pinned 2026-07-29) advertises every action's fields for ALL actions, so the model legitimately attaches `reason`/`regime_note` to an `enter` and `tradingsymbol`/`signal_id`/`strategy_id` to a `no_action`, and the authoritative discriminated union (extra=forbid) rejects exactly that — 8/13 intraday calls failed the class today; both enter signals burned all 3 retries. The 2026-08-12 half-patch (NoActionOutput accepting `confidence`/`thesis`) named this precedent and fixed one cell of the matrix. **Fix (a), pinned:** `parse_intraday` sanitizes BEFORE union validation — for a recognized `action`, drop exactly the keys in `advertised(flat schema) − fields(target model)`, log `guidance_extras_dropped`; a field the wire schema advertises can never be "genuinely foreign", while keys OUTSIDE the advertised set still die schema_invalid (R1 structural coherence intact; `engine.core.contracts` untouched; platform-stamped fields unaffected). **Fix (b):** the post-arm `tick_compact` one-shot is gated out of live sessions (trading day ∧ 08:45–15:45 IST ⇒ skip + `post_arm_skipped_in_session`): today's 11:26 mid-session crash-recovery boot fired the compaction backlog catch-up into the live session — 16 GB peak, tick processing >1 h behind wall clock by close, /db/query unresponsive, Telegram sends timing out; compaction is for idle hours, and the 22:30 slot still covers the day. **Fix (c):** pre-open broker-token validity probe (`engine.ops.token_check`, trading days 08:40 IST, no watermark): today the stale daily Kite token surfaced only at 09:50 mid-session (TokenException → FROZEN, ~108 min lost incl. a 96-min process gap); a cheap authenticated probe before open turns that into a loud "re-login before open" alert with time to act; the check itself is never load-bearing (probe errors warn, never raise). Ops action recorded: 4 zero-byte 2026-08-17 tick fragments (ABB ×2, ADANIENSOL, BAJFINANCE) quarantined 17:2x — the `tick_compact` 08-19 catch-up failure cause; the failed row retries clean on the next pass. Filed, not in this WO: Telegram "Message is too long" truncation (2 occurrences today); watchlist-change resubscribe-after-08:30-build verification on a normal day (today's outage ordering masked it). **Fix (b) closed on its second path (2026-09-04):** the in-session veto guarded only the post-arm one-shot; the 30-min catch-up sweep (ALL scope) replayed a missed `tick_compact` at 11:39 IST inside a live session (`tick_compaction_recovered 2026-09-02/NHPC`, then store stalls and late ticks for hours — the same class). `_catchup_sweep_once` now applies the identical `post_arm_exclusions` veto to every sweep pass; the vetoed job is replayed by the first pass after 15:45 or on a non-trading day, exactly as the one-shot is.

**WO-22 (2026-08-20 evening, owner-directed follow-ups from the day-one validation).** Four quality items promoted from the WO-20/21 filed list, plus three evidence investigations. **(a) `rel_volume_tod`** — the correct time-of-day-normalized participation number: today's cumulative volume ÷ median over the last 20 completed sessions of cumulative volume through the SAME elapsed minutes (1.0 = typical pace for this clock time); ≥10 valid sessions required else None; one ranged `bars_1m` read reusing the daily-history dates the legacy computation already fetches; `rel_volume` kept unchanged for continuity, the WO-20 context legend rewritten to explain both. **(b) digest saturation measured, not labelled** — `sentiment_agg` gains nullable `raw_sum`/`n_clusters` (store column-add follows the WO-5 corrections_log precedent); `_sentiment_rows` emits the unclipped total and contributing-cluster count alongside the clipped value; the context's SATURATED line renders the measurement ("raw −2.31 across 9 clusters") when present, WO-20 prose when absent (old rows). **(c) Telegram 4096-char guard** — oversized owner messages split at the send choke point on line boundaries into ≤4000-char parts with (i/N) markers, max 5 parts then an explicit truncation marker; each part inherits existing retry handling; `telegram_message_split` logged (2 messages died "Message is too long" on 2026-08-20 — an alert must never be silently lost). **(d) raw funnel counters survive restarts** — new SQLite table `funnel_raw_counts(d, strategy_id, fires)` (next migration number); the 60 s drain tick flushes changed counts (absolute UPSERT, failures warn and never raise); day-roll/first-use hydrates the in-memory counters FROM the table so restarts continue counting instead of zeroing (raw was None on 4 of the last 6 trade days' nightly funnel lines); nightly review prefers the table. **Investigations (read-only, verdicts reserved):** the 4 never-forwarded `mom` candidates of 08-13/14 (mechanism trace); the previously-filed freeze-notify dedup gap (recover the filing, map the path, count today's real 09:50/11:26 freeze notifications); the `date=1970-01-01` orphan tick partition (inventory, writer-path origin of an epoch date, compaction exposure).

**Universe expansion (2026-08-20, owner-directed — part of the zero-recommendation origination review).** `data.universe_max_watchlist` 100 → 200. With the cap at or above the eligible count, the §3.2.4 focus cap no longer binds: `EXCL_CAP` becomes structurally empty and the active watchlist IS the whole rule-passing NIFTY200 set (MIS ∩ surveillance-clean ∩ ₹5cr median traded value). Scope honesty, recorded so the change is not oversold: the batch legs (`brk20`, `ins`) and the news resolver/catalyst digest already read the full eligible set (`get_universe_eligible_symbols`, the 2026-08-18 resolver fix) — this change widens only the **per-bar scanner watchlist** (`orb`/`rsi2`/`trend`/`mom`), the momentum-universe daily preload, features `daily_snapshot`, sector map, and pre-open movers to the ~100 tail names. Costs accepted: ~2× tick volume (~1.5 GB/day compacted; A3 ticker cap 3,000 has ~15× headroom at ~205 tokens), doubled momentum-preload/features/sector-map passes, and a first-morning A2-throttled 1m backfill for the newly watched names (the 2026-08-04 50→100 precedent: a longer warm-up that morning, accepted). Pre-screen budgets deliberately UNCHANGED (`max_candidates_per_day` 48, per-strategy caps): admission is score-ranked, so widening intake raises candidate-quality competition for the same slots, not LLM spend. No code change — the knob is read by `UniverseBuilder._build` at each 08:30 build from boot-loaded settings (deploy = restart before the build).

**WO-23 (2026-08-21, from the WO-22 investigations — two safety-path notification fixes + tick-data lifecycle).** Investigation verdicts recorded first: the 4 never-forwarded `mom` candidates are NOT a defect — every `mom` publication carries `stop: null` and the deliberate 2026-07-29 stopless-unsizeable gate short-circuits them before the forward queue ("no stop ⇒ max_qty_by_risk 0 ⇒ a guaranteed no_action"); `mom` is doubly dead (no validated edge, structurally unproposable) and stays as-is. **(a) Token-rejection notify suppression — REAL, fired 2026-08-20:** `SessionManager._rejected` is one bool with two producers (the boot probe `verify_token` arms it silently by design; the mid-day breaker `on_token_rejected` dedups on it), so after a failed boot probe every subsequent GENUINE live rejection is swallowed — the 11:26:40 live TokenException produced zero owner notifications. Fix: the breaker dedups on its own `_breaker_fired` flag (set only when the hook fires, reset by `complete_login`); the boot probe no longer suppresses the breaker, so a probe-then-live sequence produces the boot login-prompt AND the breaker's distinct "entries FROZEN" critical alert (the same accepted-redundancy posture as the 08:40 `token_check`). **(b) Catch-up safety-critical freeze-notify spam guard** (IMPROVEMENT_SPEC "fires per attempt", never fixed): `_fail_safety_critical` keeps freezing unconditionally (idempotent state must always hold) but notifies once per (job_id, run_for_date) per process via a `_freeze_notified` set — a restart re-alerts once, accepted. **(c) Epoch-timestamp tick guard + retention wiring** (the `date=1970-01-01` orphan): a zeroed wire timestamp becomes `datetime.fromtimestamp(0)` and no layer checks plausibility — 103 epoch ticks were written and nightly-compacted into a 1970 partition (quarantined manually 2026-08-21 02:0x). Fix: `_wire_timestamp` rejects parsed timestamps before `_MIN_PLAUSIBLE_EXCHANGE_TS` (2020-01-01 IST) through the existing missing-timestamp drop path + `tick_timestamp_implausible` WARNING; and the designed-but-never-wired `MarketStore.apply_retention` (the WHOLE §4.5 policy: ticks 30 d, corrections 90 d, news/clusters/sentiment 1 y; defined, unit-tested, zero production callers) is invoked after each successful, non-skipped nightly compaction pass (gated on `ok ∧ ¬skipped_in_flight` — never while a concurrent run walks the partitions) — `tick_retention_applied` INFO with the per-dataset report / `tick_retention_failed` WARN, never failing the compaction job. Data begins 2026-08-03, so the first real purge lands weeks out: nothing is deleted at deploy.

**WO-24 (2026-08-21, from the first-proposal-day incidents — funnel freeze immunity + notification delivery guarantees + owner-directed dashboard).** Two live incidents on the day the funnel first produced a proposal: **(i) 09:56:40 store freeze** — at the exact moment the platform's FIRST proposal (rsi2 GVT&D, `01M0H8ZXM3PYNAVXF54A5TDGV0`) reached `_gate_and_persist`, the market-store lock was captured by the warm-up/backfill path and never released (correlated network blip: Telegram ConnectTimeout 09:56:44); feature snapshots, warmup_refresh and the gate-context read all froze simultaneously for 14 min until a recovery restart; the proposal is orphaned (persisted, never gate-judged — the §7.1 gate REMAINS unexercised) and 12 in-memory queued candidates were lost to spent day slots. **(ii) Telegram channel effectively down** — 223 ConnectTimeout send failures on 08-21 (186 on 08-20), and `_send_text` drops on failure: no retry, no queue — a recommendation fired during an outage window would be silently lost. Fixes: **(a) gate-context deadline** — `_gate_and_persist` wraps the context build in a hard timeout; on expiry the candidate fails CLOSED (infra-failure path: slot re-armed, alert, no verdict fabricated) and the drain moves on — one hung read can never freeze the funnel again. **(b) Store-lock/network separation in warm-up/backfill** — no network I/O while holding the store lock (fetch outside, write inside) + explicit timeouts on historical fetches; implementer verifies the exact hold-site first and stops-and-reports if the shape differs from the diagnosis. **(c) Orphaned-proposal sweep** — proposals past a TTL with no verdict row are surfaced loudly (`proposal_orphaned` + alert once) and counted by the nightly review; no synthetic verdicts (the audit trail stays honest). **(2026-08-24 addendum, commit `8e41747`):** the owner was paged three times over one weekend for the SAME 2026-08-21 orphan, re-announced on every boot and day-roll — an orphan older than 24h now logs INFO once per process-day instead of paging; nightly-review counting is unchanged. **(d) Notification delivery guarantees + the owner-directed dashboard section (2026-08-21):** a persisted `notifications` journal (next migration: notification_id, created_at, kind, severity, title, body, status pending/delivered/failed, attempts, delivered_at, last_error) written at notify time BEFORE any Telegram attempt — the journal IS the retry outbox (pending/failed rows re-attempted with backoff; critical kinds never expire un-alerted) AND the dashboard's data source. New bearer-authed `GET /notifications` (day-scoped, chronological) + the first actual dashboard page: a dependency-free static `web/dist` page (no build toolchain) served by the existing `_mount_dashboard_if_present` seam, showing the day's notifications chronologically with severity/delivery badges (token entered once, kept in browser localStorage — LAN-only surface, R10 API auth unchanged). Telegram connect-timeout loosened for the chronically flaky path. **(d-2, owner-directed same evening:** the notifications view moved INTO the main React dashboard as `NotificationsPanel` — last, full-width panel below news/catalyst, 60 s refresh, chronological with sticky-to-newest scroll that releases while the owner reads history, reusing the SPA's own `mt_token` auth; the standalone `/notifications-ui` page stays as a fallback; `dashboard/dist` remains untracked build output, rebuilt in place — static serving means UI deploys never touch the engine.**)** **(e) Epoch-tick drop de-escalated** — implausible-timestamp drops get their own quiet drop reason (single WARNING, no traceback, own counter; the ERROR+traceback pair fires ~16×/connect pre-market). **(f) News-analyst timeout 120→240 s** (4 timeouts in 2 days; each bills and delays scoring; batch size unchanged — one knob at a time).

**WO-D (2026-09-12, from the 2026-09-11 recommendation-drought verification — `runbooks/audit_verification_2026-09-11.md`): funnel plumbing.** The verification found the drought concentrated in plumbing, not judgement: the batch legs (brk20/hi52/cat/ins) originate only from the window-open sweep, and every in-session boot re-applies a warm-up freeze at lifecycle step 6 and re-fires the window edge, so the sweep landed inside the freeze, `on_signal_candidate` re-armed the pairs, and nothing re-published them (whole days lost 08-28, 08-31, 09-04); the forward queue is process memory and the owner restarts the engine 2–3× a session (~70 admitted candidates burned since 08-17, 210 of 520 slots never forwarded); the drain returned silently at window close; a failed analyst call kept its forward charge; the quantile ranker handed any strategy's first candidate the top band; brk20 LIMIT-at-level candidates already outside the CNC `entry_sanity_band` spent analyst slots on guaranteed rejects (6 of 15); and the flat wire schema advertised `reason` as free text while `ExitAction.reason` is a five-code Literal, so every position-event call since 09-03 failed its first attempt. Shipped, all tested: **(a) freeze-lift sweep** — a `TOPIC_RISK_STATE` subscriber re-runs the batch sweep on the FROZEN→NORMAL edge inside the trade window (`freeze_lift` takes every `window_open` branch), gated on `engine_ready` (lifts published from inside the recovery never sweep), single-flight (`_sweep_lock`; the lift QUEUES behind an in-flight sweep that already published into the freeze, every other trigger is skipped), debounced once per (day, minute) and two minutes behind a sweep that finished under NORMAL; the in-flight test is the lock's own `locked()`, never an inference from phase marks (a raising sweep must not latch the lift shut — the 2026-09-01 set-without-clear lesson). `ins` crossings are consumed only when the publication happened under NORMAL, so a freeze-lift re-sweep can still originate them. Admitted batch symbols join the ticker subscription set for the day (watchlist → held → batch → index → VIX) so the gate has a live price for eligible-but-unticked names; they never enter the warm-up coverage set. **(b) forward queue** — on the first day-roll after a boot, journal rows with `evaluated=1, forwarded=0, unsizeable=0` are re-armed (the queue they sat in died with the process); at window close the queue is flushed with re-arm once per window END (the owner moves the window intraday); an analyst call that returns no verdict refunds its forward charge and journal count (memoed per candidate per day, so the alert path cannot refund twice); WO-20d `front` entries are re-armed like any never-evaluated exit because their charge is now refunded; `_quantile_band` returns the MIDDLE band below `MIN_RANK_POPULATION = 3` (the first candidate of a strategy ranked against its own singleton population and won the top band on 09-10); a `brk20` candidate whose level sits outside the CNC band against the live LTP is DEFERRED before the claim (kept queued to its own TTL, next-best drained) rather than forwarded — a proxy for the gate rule that fails open on every unknown. **(c) exit reason** — `exit_reason` is advertised as the contract's closed enum, the sanitizer maps it onto `reason` for exits (prose moves to `thesis` only when thesis is empty), and the position-event context names the five codes; prose-only `reason` still fails validation (no heuristic mapping, R1). **(d) exit hygiene** — see the §3.6/§5.3 paragraph below and the §7.1 O16 addendum: a `holdings_observations` journal, a two-session "sold outside the ledger" predicate, one exit recommendation per session while a stop stays breached, and the gate's count exclusion. Accepted, recorded: the queue is still process memory (re-arm, not persistence); a day FROZEN through the close is not flushed (the freeze gate precedes the window check by design); the orphan sweep also re-arms governor-blocked drops (bounded at 2–3 restarts); a renamed tradingsymbol reads held 0 forever and would silence its position events after two sessions (bounded by the daily §3.6 alert, the day-plan block and the max-holding sweep); `RecommendationPipeline._positions_summary` (analyst headroom context) still lists positions the journal says are gone.

**WO-25 (2026-08-25 overnight, from the 2026-08-24 full-day incident — the late-tick death spiral + notification queue repair).** The day's evidence, measured: once bar-builder processing slips past the late-grace window, EVERY tick takes the expensive late path and throughput drops to ~0.5× real-time — the lag then grows unboundedly (20 min behind by 12:42 with 212,338 per-tick `late_tick_past_grace` INFO lines; after a 12:45 clean restart it re-tipped after ~13:25 and was NINE HOURS behind by midnight, starving all EOD jobs — zero `job_runs` rows for 08-24's ins_crossings/tick_compact/nightly_review). A 35-min clean-boot watch at full 202-symbol Monday volume showed ZERO late ticks — steady-state capacity is fine; the spiral is the defect (Wednesday 08-20's afternoon collapse was the same spiral, tipped by in-session compaction). **(a)** in-range late ticks (212k/212k of yesterday's) become O(memory): the builder retains the last 5 completed bars per symbol and serves the range check from memory — store I/O only for a genuinely-amending tick; per-tick logging replaced by one line per (symbol, minute) + a per-minute `late_ticks_summary`; a lag watchdog (`tick_processing_lagging` at 120 s, once per 5 min, recovery line) makes the next slip visible in minutes, not via a slow dashboard. **(b)** Notification transport + queue: explicit HTTPX timeouts (connect 20 s — api.telegram.org first-connects MEASURED at 4.25/4.84 s against the library's 5 s default: the mass-failure mechanism); the outbox drainer selects retry-ELIGIBLE rows (backoff-elapsed) ordered critical-first then chronological — a 203-deep queue of expiring spam head-of-line-blocked a fresh owner ack at attempt 1 for hours. **(c)** Alert-source episode dedup: health problem-set alerts fire on CHANGE (+ one repeat per 30 min while unchanged, recovery announced once — 57 identical `feed_stale` alerts in one morning built the queue); agent-failure owner alerts throttle per (agent, reason) episode with success reset. Deployed overnight with the boot that also catches up 08-24's missed EOD chain.

**WO-26 (2026-08-25 afternoon — the starvation class closed at the root + the ticker supervisor's three defects).** The 08-25 session ran EMPTY (zero candidates evaluated in the owner's window) while ticks/bars/flushes all flowed: the tick-flush pile-up saturated the shared default executor for ~4.4 h (watchdog probe pending 09:15→13:39, consecutive=264) and starved every store read the intelligence layer needed. **(a) Flush single-flight with skip** — a flush finding another in flight returns immediately (`flush_skipped_in_flight`, skip counter as the alertable signal); the one caller that legitimately needs blocking (`close()`'s final flush) gets a bounded 15 s wait. **(b) Executor isolation** — MarketStore owns a 4-worker `mt-store` pool for all `a*` wrappers and a dedicated 1-thread `mt-flush` pool; zero `asyncio.to_thread` remains in the store: disk writes starving the intelligence layer is now impossible by construction. Steady-state flushes make zero partition-dir filesystem calls (per-day dir cache). **(c) Watchdog re-probe** — a pending store probe re-issues fresh every 5th pulse with abandoned-probe accounting (`abandoned=`); 08-25's "pending forever, zero new information" is structurally impossible. **(d) Ticker supervisor, three defects from the recurring false-STALE wedge (4 documented multi-hour freezes, incl. 03:37→08:30 on 08-25):** (i) liveness was heartbeat-frames-only and stamped on the same starved loop — now EVERY frame stamps liveness (a tick IS a heartbeat) and the monitor discounts its own wake-up overshoot before declaring silence (ages stay wall-clock; only the verdict is discounted, `ticker_silence_discounted_loop_starved`); (ii) STALE was a one-way door (no transition out except respawn) — now any frame recovers STALE→HEALTHY (`feed_stale_recovered`); (iii) `kill_after_s` was a LIE that deadlocked: `_respawn` cancelled the read loop first, whose cleanup awaits `server.wait_closed()` — which under CPython ≥3.12 waits for the child connection to drop — while `_terminate_child`, the only dropper, sat behind the same lock (reproduced standalone; every wedge released at the exact instant of an unrelated `ticker_link_closed`). Fixed by terminate-child-FIRST ordering in `_respawn` and `stop()`, plus a 5 s bound on `server.wait_closed()`. Discrimination proofs pinned: restoring each old rule reproduces its incident. The supervisor now stops MISDIAGNOSING upstream stalls; (a)/(b) remove the stall source itself.

**WO-27 (2026-08-26 — the scan hot path stops reading the database).** The 09:36 stall-watchdog stack dump caught the starvation class's SECOND entrance: the per-bar scan path calls SYNC store methods directly from shared-pool threads (bypassing WO-26's `mt-store` isolation, which covers only the async wrappers) — `intraday_snapshot` re-read the catalyst watchlist + sentiment agg + theme map + sector map per SNAPSHOT (data that changes a few times a day, queried hundreds of times a minute), plus a ~7,500-row `rel_volume_tod` history read per bar per symbol; one slow open-time query serialized every scan behind the prescreen lock (episodes oscillated stall→recover ~2 min — chokepoint, not wedge; bars stayed current). Fix: **(a)** a TTL'd day-context cache (`_SENTIMENT_CACHE_TTL_S` 60 s, one combined 6-statement read per TTL, digest as-of folded in — no separate probe, day-change invalidation, double-checked locking); **(b)** DAY caches (not TTL — the underlying 20 sessions are completed/immutable) for the per-symbol daily window and the `rel_volume_tod` cumulative-volume curves (array-based prefix sums + bisect, ~12 B/bar); **(c)** today's tape is never cached — the scan provider hands the engine the in-memory series it already holds (`bars=`), safe because BarBuilder persists before publishing. Contract, test-pinned by spying the store's lowest-level `_execute`: a steady-state snapshot performs ZERO DuckDB reads and exactly one INSERT (the §4.3 audit-chain snapshot write, deliberately kept). `daily_snapshot` shares the same context read, so both snapshot surfaces see one consistent digest run.

**WO-28 (2026-08-26, the platform's first live gate verdicts).** All three of the day's first `per_trade_risk` verdicts rejected: the analyst's own sizing quote (`RecommendationPipeline._max_qty_by_risk`) omitted the §7.1 `overnight_gap_mult` (2.5×) the gate charges every swing/position unit risk, so proposals arrived quoted ~2.5× oversized (MOTHERSON qty 60 vs a true budget of 24; HAL qty 2 vs a true 0) — the quote now mirrors the gate's formula exactly, so analyst proposals are pre-bounded by the same number §7.1 enforces (commit `fdf360b`). The same pass gave `rsi2`/`trend`/`mom` a gate note: no configured edge means C3 needs an explicit `target_price` or the enter is unverifiable (§6.1 contract block, WO-20).

**WO-29 (2026-08-26 evening, owner-directed after the FIRST executed recommendation): owner commands speak ticker, not rec_id.** The first live `/taken` exposed that `rec_id` is internal plumbing never shown to the owner — the §3.6 delivery message carried no usable handle, making the §8.3 G2 flow (`/taken`–`/closed`) impossible without an intermediary. Fix: **(a)** `/taken`, `/closed` and the decline command resolve their first argument as a TICKER against the owner's open recommendations (delivered, un-actioned, un-expired; case-insensitive): exactly one match ⇒ proceed; several ⇒ reply listing them (symbol, side, delivered time, short id) and act on none; none ⇒ reply with the current open list. A 26-char ULID first argument keeps the original id path (tickers — GVT&D, M&M, BAJAJ-AUTO — are never ULID-shaped); **(b)** the §3.6 recommendation message gains a copy-ready footer with the resolved commands (`record: /taken <SYMBOL> <qty> <price> · decline: /veto <SYMBOL>`) so the delivery teaches the reply. Owner identity/actor semantics, veto-vs-taken distinction, and all downstream recording are unchanged — this renames the handle, not the contract.

All signals respect the universe (trading scope), the cost gate (expected edge ≥ 2× breakeven, C3 [tunable; envelope row `edge_multiple_min`]), and every §7.1 limit. At ₹20,000 the practical book is 1–3 concurrent positions — `mom.top_n` defaults to 2 (C4: never ₹2,000 × 10 scrips). `cat` is deliberately **swing-only and long-only** at introduction (O12 horizon; §1.4.9 shorts gate); an MIS same-day variant is deferred (§13). Its news dependence flows through the single persisted watchlist artifact, so the §9 replay/backtest path stays fully reproducible from persisted scores — no LLM in the replay loop (R8/§9.6).

### 6.2 Feature set (deterministic, versioned; `feature_set_version` stamped everywhere)

- **Price/volatility:** returns (1/5/20 d), ATR(14) 1m+1d, realized vol 20d, gap stats, distance to 20/50/200-DMA, day range position.
- **Volume/liquidity:** 20d median traded value, relative volume, spread estimate from depth snapshots, cumulative-delta-derived bar volume features (A13).
- **Market context:** NIFTY/sector index returns + trend state, India VIX level/Δ (E5), advance-decline, expiry-day flag, results-day flag, ex-date proximity (A12), surveillance status (A8), `flagged_instrument_day` (block deals).
- **Sentiment / catalyst (features v2 — `feature_set_version` bumps to 2 when the §2.7 layer lands, §8.3):** decay-weighted sentiment at **symbol, sector, theme, and market** level from `sentiment_agg` (§2.7 step 5; half-life `cat.decay_halflife_h`), plus per-symbol catalyst fields `{on_watchlist, grade, event_type, event_age_h, materiality, source_domain_count}` and a boolean **`sentiment_available`** — all **untrusted, feature-only** (§2.4): they feed River and LLM context; only the `cat` scanner's watchlist seam may originate from them (O11, §2.4 item 4). **Absent-news defaults are pinned (so a news outage cannot shift non-`cat` candidate ranking — chaos case 20):** sentiment fields = 0, `on_watchlist` = false, `materiality` = 0, `sentiment_available` = false — an in-distribution "no news" vector, never missing/NaN. **Bump mechanics:** on the v2 bump, the §6.4 trial-count window N resets per `feature_set_version`; River challengers train on v2-stamped rows, including pre-v2 rows only under explicit neutral-fill (the same defaults above) — and every post-bump model still enters via the normal §6.4 challenger gate.
- **Microstructure (intraday):** opening-range stats, VWAP distance, last-30-bar pattern summary.

### 6.3 Learnable-parameter envelope (R4) — **Tier-2-owned `config/envelope.yaml`, hash-verified (§2.4); nothing outside this table is learnable**

| Parameter | Range | Default | Used by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `orb.orb_minutes` | 15–45 | 30 | orb |
| `orb.vol_mult` | 1.2–3.0 | 1.5 | orb |
| `orb.stop_range_frac` | 0.5–1.0 | 1.0 | orb (v2 2026-07-12 — replaces `stop_atr_mult`; stop anchors to the opposite opening-range edge, see §6.1) |
| `orb.rr_target` | 1.0–3.0 | 1.5 | orb |
| `rsi2.rsi_entry` | 2–15 | 10 | rsi2 |
| `rsi2.rsi_exit` | 50–80 | 65 | rsi2 |
| `rsi2.stop_pct` | 2.0–6.0 | 4.0 | rsi2 |
| `rsi2.max_hold_days` | 3–15 | 10 | rsi2 |
| `trend.adx_min` | 15–30 | 20 | trend |
| `trend.trail_atr_mult` | 1.5–4.0 | 2.5 | trend |
| `mom.top_n` | 1–3 | 2 | mom |
| `mom.rebalance_days` | 10–20 | 15 | mom (upper bound = §7.1 swing 20-td holding cap; envelope ranges must lie within §7.1 limits — asserted by a CI test) |
| `cat.materiality_min` | 0.55–0.85 | 0.70 | cat (watchlist `originating` threshold, §2.7 step 5) |
| `cat.confirm_move_pct` | 0.5–3.0 (%) | 1.0 | cat (price-confirmation move vs prior close) |
| `cat.confirm_vol_mult` | 1.2–3.0 | 1.5 | cat (relative-volume confirmation) |
| `cat.stop_atr_mult` | 1.0–2.5 | 1.5 | cat |
| `cat.rr_target` | 1.0–3.0 | 1.5 | cat |
| `cat.max_hold_days` | 3–10 | 5 | cat (≤ §7.1 swing 20-td cap; CI-asserted) |
| `cat.max_event_age_days` | 1–3 | 2 | cat (catalyst staleness bound — **counted in trading sessions from cluster `first_seen`**, §2.7 step 5) |
| `cat.novelty_min` | 0.3–0.8 | 0.5 | cat (freshness condition for `originating` grade, §2.7 step 5 — a behaviour param like its `cat.*` siblings, NOT part of the owner-only guard block) |
| `cat.decay_halflife_h` | 12–48 | 24 | sentiment_agg decay (§2.7 step 5 / §6.2 features v2) |
| `signal_quality_threshold` | 0.55–0.80 | 0.60 | River filter (§6.4); River output **calibrated to historical hit-rate before thresholding**, range re-derived from Phase-1/2 data |
| `edge_multiple_min` | 1.5–3.0 | 2.0 | cost gate (C3) — the **only** learner-movable gate knob |

> **Per-strategy publication caps are owner-only (WO-1, 2026-08-13).** `strategy.prescreen.max_per_strategy_day` (and `max_candidates_per_day`, and `admission_mode`) live in `settings.yaml`, **not** in this table, and are never learner-movable. The reason is structural rather than conservative: those caps decide *which strategies get evaluated at all*, so a learner able to move them could starve every strategy but its current favourite and then present the resulting one-sided ledger as evidence for moving them further — a self-reinforcing loop with the attribution data it feeds on. The platform may *suggest* a split in the nightly review; only the owner sets one. The same reasoning is why the §7.1 `catalyst_guard` block below is fenced off.

**Explicitly NOT learnable (Tier-2/owner-only, R4):** every §7.1 risk limit, the leverage cap (envelope is [1×, cap], cap moves only by owner phase-gate approval — trading scope), kill-switch config, budget allocations, mode gates, the bounds of this table itself, **and the entire §7.1 `catalyst_guard` block (min source domains, long-direction sentiment floor, per-day catalyst entry cap, originating event types, digest staleness bound) — it is the anti-manipulation surface (§2.4 item 4/§2.7) and must never be learner-movable even though the adjacent `cat.*` behaviour parameters are.** The joint envelope-floor state of those behaviour params (materiality 0.55, move 0.5%, vol 1.2×, novelty 0.3, age 3 td — all reachable together via legitimate promotions) was **adversarially reviewed 2026-07-06 and judged safe**: every manipulation-specific guard (corroboration, event-type exclusion, surveillance/liquidity universe, per-day cap, shrink-only fixed sizing) is independent of them and non-learnable, so softening the behaviour legs weakens signal quality, not the security boundary. **Deliberate carve-out (the only one):** `edge_multiple_min` is a gate-enforced *strategy-quality* threshold the gate reads from `envelope_state` (§6.5), learner-movable strictly inside the hash-verified bounds above; the §7.1 preamble restates this exception. **`analyst_confidence_min` is NOT learner-movable** — it is owner-only (gate-enforced, defined here in §6.3 / dashboard §3.2.11; it has no §7.1 limit-table row; **starting value 0.55, stored top-level in `limits.yaml` — inside the protected store but outside the `limits:` rule map**), calibrated to historical hit-rate, and changed via the dashboard parameter-suggest/approve surface: the nightly reviewer proposes a value *with reasoning/numbers*, the owner verifies and applies (§5.5/§3.2.11). This generalises to every owner-only tunable — the platform may *suggest*, only the owner *sets*.

### 6.4 Online learning + champion/challenger lifecycle (O4, E3, R4)

- **Online model:** a River incremental classifier (logistic regression baseline; ARF as challenger class) predicting P(signal reaches target before stop | features), trained on ledger outcomes as they close. Used only as a *filter/rank* on pre-screen candidates via `signal_quality_threshold` — it cannot size, place, or modify anything; its output is **calibrated to historical hit-rate before thresholding** (§6.3). **The LIVE-serving model is a frozen version, not the continuously-updated learner (R4 promotion invariant):** retraining runs continuously in the background, but each retrained model is a *challenger* that must pass the shadow/validation gate (steps 3–4) before it replaces the live-serving version — the live model never silently swaps mid-cycle. **Training population is split by `is_paper`:** the live-serving model trains only on live-routed outcomes (paper/RECOMMEND rows are a separate model or a routing feature), so paper/live distribution shift can't contaminate live serving. Drift detection (ADWIN) on feature and outcome streams; drift alarm ⇒ live model demoted to the last-good frozen version + alert (E3). **Across an engine-off gap (§2.6), ADWIN windows are session-keyed and fed the backfilled gap-session outcomes in chronological session order on startup catch-up (same source as the promotion evaluator) — so a wall-clock offline discontinuity neither spuriously fires nor masks drift.**
- **Lifecycle (every change, incl. envelope-parameter moves):**
  1. **Propose** — nightly reviewer suggestion, scheduled re-optimization, or River retrain. **The trial count N counts *every internally-evaluated configuration* (the full sweep/grid cardinality), not just the surfaced `param_sets` candidate rows** — the optimizer reports the number of configs it scored, so a sweep that tries 200 grid points cannot log N=1 and keep the bar lenient (closes the gaming hole). Every *surfaced* candidate is still logged as a `param_sets` row (`status='candidate'`, `evaluated_at`) for audit; the deflating N is the evaluated-config count (E2 multiple-testing discipline — the AI both generates and selects, so untracked trials would silently inflate any expectancy).
  2. **Validate (E2)** — anchored walk-forward (6 m train / 1 m test, rolling) **and** CPCV via skfolio (purge 5 d, embargo 5 d) on cost-adjusted returns (C3). The `ValidationReport` **must cite the trial count N** (the evaluated-config count from step 1, windowed — *not* the count of surfaced `param_sets` rows) — a report with no cited N is not promotable (E2). Pass: out-of-sample expectancy > 0 after costs in **≥`fold_pass_min(N)`** of CPCV folds and max DD ≤ 1.25× champion's, where `fold_pass_min` **tightens monotonically with N** [tunable]: 60% for N ≤ 10, 70% for 11 ≤ N ≤ 30, 80% for N > 30 — a lightweight deflation that raises the out-of-sample bar with the number of things tried. **N is windowed over a rolling lookback (or reset per `feature_set_version`), not a never-resetting lifetime count** — so a mature strategy isn't permanently frozen at the strictest bar, and a stale strategy can't keep a lenient one. The quarantined final holdout is **consumed once and rolled forward to a fresh out-of-sample slice — never re-used across candidates** (re-using it N times turns it into a selection set and biases out-of-sample expectancy). (Full Deflated-Sharpe(N)/PBO/MinTRL machinery is deliberately deferred at ₹20k sample sizes — owner-chosen, §12 / §14; the seam to add it is this same cited-N report.)
     - **Backtest semantics the validated returns MUST be produced under (amended 2026-08-13, WO-2 — every report generated before this date is superseded):**
       - **Fill timing — next bar's OPEN, never the signal bar's close.** A signal is computed from bar *t*'s completed values, so the earliest fill it can obtain is bar *t+1*'s open. Implementation: the signal frames are shifted one bar forward and an explicit price frame (the open) is passed to the vectorbt backtest; signals on the last bar of the frame are dropped, and for intraday (`orb`) the shift is session-aware — a signal on a session's last bar is dropped rather than carried into the next session, with the one exception of the forced MIS session-end square-off (a clock-driven obligation that stays on its own session's last bar). Stops/targets anchor at the FILL price. *(The defect this replaces: vectorbt's default `price=None` resolves to the SAME row's close, so every rsi2/trend/mom/orb signal filled at the very close that generated it.)* The same rule governs a strategy's ranking inputs: a cross-sectional rank that fills on session *t+1* must be computed from closes through *t* (`mom`) — the ranking window never includes the session traded, which is the live `ScanContext` window (bars through *d−1*, traded on *d*).
       - **Cost surface — statutory fees AND the measured bid-ask spread.** Costs come from `CostModel` (C1–C4), which since WO-2 charges `costs.yaml spread_pct` as a separate `spread` component on top of the statutory fees: half the quoted spread per leg, so a round trip pays the full measured spread. Backtests charge fees via the per-side fee constant and spread via per-order slippage (never both through one number); the live §7.1 `min_viable_size` viability check inherits it automatically because it reads `CostModel.round_trip`/`breakeven_pct` — which strictly tightens the gate. The measured value and its provenance are in §14 Q17. `spread_pct` is **plain owner-movable config, deliberately NOT in the §6.3 envelope** — it is a measurement of the market, not a learnable strategy knob (R4).
       - **Sizing — sweeps trade at the reference notional.** Per-symbol backtest cash = `reference_notional` (₹20,000, the live per-trade notional), so the constant per-side fee is calibrated at the size actually traded.
     - **Margin floor (added 2026-08-13, WO-3):** a fold-pass FRACTION says how OFTEN the edge was positive, never by how much — four rsi2 runs passed at exactly 12/15 = 80.0% with every passing split under 0.02%/day (median ≈ 0.0006%/day). Promotion therefore ALSO requires **median passing-split expectancy ≥ `cost_floor / 20` per day**, where `cost_floor` is the strategy's full round-trip friction (fees + spread) at the sweep's sizing. The **20** is the §7.1 swing holding cap / the §6.3 `rebalance_days` upper bound: *a position must, over the horizon it is actually held, earn at least the one round trip it costs to hold it*. On the post-WO-2 CNC ₹20k surface (0.2992% fees + 0.0200% spread = 0.3192%) the floor is **0.01596%/day** — the recorded rsi2 medians fail it by ~27×, and an edge netting one round trip per 20-session hold sits exactly on it. Fail-closed: no cost floor supplied ⇒ not promotable (a floor that silently skips is no floor). The `fold_pass_fraction` comparison stays **strict-<** — moving a documented boundary silently is a semantics trap; the margin floor is the real fix.
     - **Holding-horizon-scaled floor — PRE-REGISTERED 2026-09-12 (R2), evidence only, wires nothing:** the **20** above is the §7.1 *swing* cap; applied to a **positional** leg it overstates the per-day bar by up to 6×. `trend` is positional — `limits.yaml max_holding.position_trading_days: 120`, and the rule itself (20/50 EMA golden cross ∧ ADX > `adx_min`, exit on death cross or the `trail_atr_mult × ATR` trail) carries no time target — and the 2026-08-14 corrected run passed **93.3% of CPCV folds** against a 60% bar at N=9, failing **only** this floor (median passing split 0.01034%/day vs 0.01596%/day). **Registered before the run:** for `trend` the floor is `cost_floor / 120` = 0.3192% / 120 = **0.00266%/day**; *everything else identical to the 08-14 protocol* — 2024-01-01→2025-12-31, coarse grid (N=9), next-session-open fills, statutory fees + the measured spread, ₹20,000 per-symbol sizing, the same 200-name universe, `fold_pass_min(9)` = 60%, CPCV purge 5 d / embargo 5 d, the coarse↔medium adjacency flag. **One additional REPORTING-ONLY cell** over **2024-01-01→2026-09-11** (daily bars now reach 2026-09-11), reported beside the primary cell and **never pooled with it** — pooling would re-spend the 2024–2025 slice inside a longer window and pass the same evidence off as more of it. **Decision rule, fixed before the run: promotable iff the unchanged house rule passes with the 120-session denominator — no other threshold moves, and a NOT-PROMOTABLE result is the deliverable if that is what comes back.** A pass changes nothing live: registering an edge is an owner §8.6 decision, and the denominator is a CLI argument (`scripts/backtest.py --margin-floor-days`, default 20) that wires nothing into the engine — `MARGIN_FLOOR_DAYS` stays 20 for every other caller. **POPULATION — SURVIVORSHIP-TAINTED PROXY, stated here because it is uncorrectable and must not live only in a chat reply.** The 200-name universe is the 2026-07-12 `orb`-sweep snapshot (`data/reports/orb_sweep_20260712T033313.json`, the canonical `$syms` list in `runbooks/COMMANDS.md`) applied **backwards** over 2024→2026. NIFTY-200 membership as of each trade date is stored nowhere in this platform, so a name that entered the index after a big run reads as eligible throughout that run, and a name delisted inside the window is simply absent — the same defect, in the same words, as the `brk20` (R1) and `hi52` registrations in §6.1. It biases the **level** (per-trade expectancy, total return) optimistically by an amount this platform cannot measure; it does **not** touch the **comparison** with the 2026-08-14 run, which used a byte-identical list. From 2026-09-12 every sweep and validation artifact carries the caveat in its notes and `population_is_survivorship_tainted_proxy: true` in its JSON, so the warning travels with the numbers instead of behind them. **RUN 2026-09-12 12:14 IST (engine Stopped and verified before the run; `scripts/backtest.py trend --from … --index-symbol "NIFTY 50" --symbols $syms --margin-floor-days 120`, the same 200-name list; cell A `data/reports/trend_20260912T121442.{md,json}` + `trend_sweep_20260912T121432.md`, cell B `trend_20260912T121540.{md,json}` + `trend_sweep_20260912T121522.md`. This supersedes the 01:07 first pass, which produced identical statistics but recorded no holding period; the 9-row sweep table is byte-identical to the 2026-08-14 run, so the mechanics did not move.)** **The pre-registered rule returns PASS, and the manager's reading is still NOT A PROMOTION CANDIDATE.** Both are true and neither is softened.

       - **It passes — at the CAP.** Cell A: CPCV **93.3%** of folds against the 60% bar at N=9, median passing split **0.01034%/day** against the registered **0.00266%/day** floor = **3.89×**. Cell B (reporting-only, never pooled): 86.7% and 0.00740%/day = 2.78×. The same run at the DEFAULT 20-session floor still reproduces the 2026-08-14 NOT-PROMOTABLE verdict character-for-character, so nothing about the standing protocol moved.
       - **The denominator is a cap the backtest never enforces, and the horizon is now measured.** 120 is `max_holding.position_trading_days`; `_signals_trend` emits no time exit, so no trade was ever truncated at it. Cell A's realized hold, reported for the first time by this run: median **33** sessions, mean **48.6**, p90 121 over 303 trades — **30 of which were still OPEN** at the window edge (closed-only: median 31, mean 45.1). Re-basing the SAME round-trip floor on those horizons: **1.07× at the realized median**, **1.58× at the mean** (1.00× / 1.46× closed-only). Cell B is worse and **fails outright at its own realized median** — 0.72× at 31 sessions, 1.07× at its 46.1-session mean. A pass quoted as "3.9× over the floor" and a pass that is 1.0–1.6× over it at the horizon the strategy is actually held are not the same claim, and only the second one is about this strategy.
       - **Two sweep-accounting biases run in the strategy's favour** — daily-frame stops evaluated on the close, and open trades inside the per-trade expectancy (the headline +3.626%/trade is +1.841% over closed round trips alone). Both are itemised in the sweep-mechanics work order below, both point the same way, and neither is fixed yet.
       - **Verdict as recorded.** The decision rule was fixed in writing before the run and it returns **PASS**; a rule pre-registered to close a multiplicity hole does not get re-read after the result is in, so PASS is what this record says. The **manager's reading is that `trend` is NOT a promotion candidate** until the sweep-mechanics work order lands and both cells are re-run on the corrected mechanics: the pass is marginal at the measured horizon, it fails at cell B's, the level it is measured at is survivorship-tainted, and the two accounting biases all push the same direction. Nothing is wired either way — registering `trend` as an edge remains an owner §8.6 decision, `MARGIN_FLOOR_DAYS` is still 20, and no envelope, limit, gate or kill path moved. *(All three items — the two named here plus the unspread stop exit — were fixed on 2026-09-13 and both cells re-run; see the WO-M result under the sweep-mechanics work order below. This paragraph is left as the 2026-09-12 record and its numbers are never pooled with the corrected ones.)*
     - **Sweep-mechanics work order — PRE-REGISTERED 2026-09-12 (R2 follow-up).
